<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We resolve the three-dimensional Ball–Evans approximation problem for $`1\le p\le2`$. Every $`W^{1,p}`$ homeomorphism between arbitrary bounded domains in ℝ<sup>3</sup> is a strong $`W^{1,p}`$ limit of smooth diffeomorphisms onto the same target.

- **claim-fixed-target-diffeomorphic-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-theorem)): Let $\Omega,\Lambda\subset\mathbb R^3$ be nonempty bounded connected open sets, let $1\le p\le2$, and let $f:\Omega\to\Lambda$ be a homeomorphism in $W^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb R^3)$. There exist $C^\infty$ diffeomorphisms $f_j:\Omega\to\Lambda$, each belonging to $W^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb R^3)$, such that $$\label{main:convergence} \int_\Omega\bigl( \|f_j-f\|^p+\|Df_j-Df\|^p\bigr)\,dx\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Strong approximation controls both values and derivatives while each smooth diffeomorphism has exactly the original target. Boundary regularity and inverse Sobolev regularity are not additional hypotheses.
- **claim-positive-local-tolerances** (construction, [source](#stmt-pre-local-tolerances)): Let $U\subset\mathbb R^n$ be open and let $a:U\to(0,\infty)$ be continuous. For every $L>0$ there is a positive $L$-Lipschitz function $d$ on $U$ such that $$d(x)\le \min\{a(x),1,L\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,\mathbb R^n\setminus U)\}.$$ There are also positive smooth minorants satisfying prescribed positive continuous upper bounds on their values and first derivatives. One may additionally impose positive constant bounds on a locally finite family of compact regional tests. Finite collections of such requirements can be combined, and error budgets on a countable compact exhaustion can be made summable.
  - Explanation: Positive minorants combine local value and derivative restrictions with summable regional errors.
- **claim-strong-fine-smoothing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-smoothing)): Let $\Omega,\Omega'\subset\mathbb R^3$ be bounded domains, let $1\le p<\infty$, and let $H:\Omega\to\Omega'$ be a locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism in $W^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb R^3)$. For every $\varepsilon>0$ and every continuous function $\xi:\Omega\to(0,\infty)$, there is a $C^\infty$ diffeomorphism $g:\Omega\to\Omega'$ such that $$\label{sm:smoothing-conclusion} \left\lVert g-H\right\rVert_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}<\varepsilon, \qquad \|g(x)-H(x)\|<\xi(x)\quad(x\in\Omega).$$ In particular, $g$ belongs to $W^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb R^3)$, and the approximations have exactly the target $\Omega'$.
  - Explanation: A locally bi-Lipschitz Sobolev homeomorphism admits smooth diffeomorphic approximation with strong error control and a prescribed positive continuous pointwise tolerance.
- **claim-relative-pl-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-sm-relative-pl)): Let $M,N$ be PL three-manifolds without boundary, with $N$ metrized, and let $f:M\to N$ be a homeomorphism. Suppose that $K\subset M$ is closed and that $f$ is PL on an open neighborhood of $K$. Given a continuous function $\eta:M\to(0,\infty)$, there is a PL homeomorphism $f_1:M\to N$ which agrees with $f$ on a neighborhood of $K$ and satisfies $$d_N(f_1(x),f(x))<\eta(x)\qquad(x\in M).$$ Noncompact manifolds are allowed. For manifolds with boundary the same statement holds if $K$ contains $\partial M$ and $f$ is PL near $K$.
  - Explanation: Fine PL approximation preserves the map near the closed region where it is already PL. The boundary version requires the protected region to contain the manifold boundary.
- **claim-small-ambient-extension** (construction, [source](#stmt-sm-local-extension)): Fix a closed Euclidean cube $B\subset\mathbb R^3$, compact sets $C,D\subset B$ with $C\subset\operatorname{int}B$, and an open neighborhood $O$ of $C\cup D\cup\partial B$. For every $\lambda>0$ there is a $\delta>0$, depending only on these source sets and on $\lambda$, with the following property. If $e:O\to\mathbb R^3$ is an embedding, $$\sup_{x\in O}\|e(x)-x\|<\delta, \qquad e=\operatorname{id}\ \hbox{on }D\cup\partial B,$$ then there is a homeomorphism $A:\mathbb R^3\to\mathbb R^3$ such that $$\label{sm:extension-conclusion} A=e\ \hbox{on }C,\qquad A=\operatorname{id}\ \hbox{on }D\cup(\mathbb R^3\setminus\operatorname{int}B), \qquad \sup_{\mathbb R^3}\|A-\operatorname{id}\|<\lambda.$$ The number $\delta$ is uniform over a finite list of fixed normalized configurations $(B,C,D,O)$. No derivative bound on $e$ is required.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently small embedding near the prescribed source configuration extends to a small ambient homeomorphism fixing the protected set and the exterior of the cube. This gives displacement control without a derivative estimate.
- **claim-proper-degree-fixed-target** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-proper-degree)): Let $h:\Omega\to\Omega'$ be an orientation-preserving homeomorphism between bounded domains, and let $g:\Omega\to\mathbb R^3$ be a smooth map with $\det Dg>0$. If $$\label{sm:target-distance} \|g(x)-h(x)\|<\frac12\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(h(x),\mathbb R^3\setminus\Omega') \qquad(x\in\Omega),$$ then $g$ is a diffeomorphism of $\Omega$ onto $\Omega'$.
  - Explanation: Positive local Jacobian and pointwise proximity relative to target distance give a proper homotopy of degree one. The degree count forces a unique preimage of every target point.
- **claim-strong-pl-smoothing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-pl-smoothing)): The conclusion of Theorem <a href="#sm:smoothing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sm:smoothing">3</a> holds if $H$ is replaced by a locally finite PL homeomorphism $h:\Omega\to\Omega'$ in $W^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb R^3)$.
  - Explanation: A locally finite PL Sobolev homeomorphism admits the same fine strong smoothing and exact-target conclusion as a locally bi-Lipschitz input.
- **claim-adapted-dyadic-meshes** (construction, [source](#stmt-sm-mesh)): There is an absolute integer $n_0$ with the following property. Given arbitrary positive local upper bounds on the fine scale, there is a locally finite dyadic cube decomposition $\mathcal Q$ of $\Omega$ with centers $c_Q$ and side lengths $l_Q$ for which, on writing $$\label{sm:patch-box} B_Q(s)=c_Q+[-s l_Q,s l_Q]^3,$$ the following hold:* 1. *$B_Q(100)\Subset\Omega$, and cubes meeting $B_Q(50)$ have side lengths comparable to $l_Q$ by absolute constants.* 2. *The normalized cube configurations in $B_Q(6)$ belong to a finite list. There is a conforming affine triangulation of $\mathcal Q$ with finitely many normalized nondegenerate shapes on these configurations.* 3. *The cubes have $n_0$ colors so that the closed boxes $B_Q(6)$ of a single color are pairwise disjoint.* 4. *Attach to $Q$ a coarse index $P(Q)$ containing $c_Q$, using a fixed convention on coarse boundaries. The initial scale bounds can be reduced so that all interactions through any fixed number of neighboring patch layers are confined to a fixed locally finite graph of coarse indices, independent of further mesh refinement. The corresponding patches lie in the neighborhoods on which the required bounds in Equation <a href="#sm:coarse-bilip" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:coarse-bilip">[sm:coarse-bilip]</a> hold.* *The upper bounds on the fine scale remain arbitrarily prescribable.
  - Explanation: An arbitrarily fine locally finite mesh has finitely many normalized configurations, a conforming finite shape library, disjoint color classes, and interactions confined to fixed coarse neighborhoods.
- **claim-protected-core-insertion** (construction, [source](#stmt-sm-insertion)): Consider a patch $Q$ and a finite collection of older protected boxes with the relative sizes and positions provided by Lemma <a href="#sm:mesh" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sm:mesh">9</a>. For every older box prescribe a larger half-shrunk box and a smaller fully-shrunk box, separated by a fixed positive normalized margin. Let $G:\Omega\to\Omega'$ be a homeomorphism, and let $h_Q$ be a PL embedding on a neighborhood of $B_Q(3)$. Assume that $h_Q=G$ on the intersection of $B_Q(3)$ with each half-shrunk box.* *For every $\lambda>0$ there is an $a>0$, depending only on $\lambda$, the normalized source configuration, and a local constant $K_P$ in Equation <a href="#sm:coarse-bilip" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:coarse-bilip">[sm:coarse-bilip]</a>, such that $$\label{sm:insertion-smallness} \sup_{B_Q(5)}\|G-H\|<a l_Q, \qquad \sup_{B_Q(3)}\|h_Q-H\|<a l_Q$$ implies the following conclusion. There is a domain homeomorphism $A$ supported in $B_Q(5)$, with $$\sup\|A-\operatorname{id}\|<\lambda l_Q,$$ such that $G\circ A=h_Q$ on a neighborhood of $B_Q(2)$ and $G\circ A=G$ on the fully-shrunk older boxes. The tolerances are uniform over a finite list of source configurations. They do not depend on the derivatives of $G$ or of $h_Q$.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently close PL patch can be installed by a small domain homeomorphism while retaining smaller older protected cores. The input tolerances depend on source geometry and the reference map, rather than replacement derivatives.
- **claim-strong-bilip-pl-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sm-bilip-pl)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#sm:smoothing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sm:smoothing">3</a>, there is a locally finite PL homeomorphism $G:\Omega\to\Omega'$ satisfying $$\left\lVert G-H\right\rVert_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}<\varepsilon, \qquad \|G(x)-H(x)\|<\xi(x)\quad(x\in\Omega).$$
  - Explanation: Finite local PL model libraries fix derivative bounds before mesh refinement makes the exceptional volume small, yielding strong approximation without circular error choices.
- **claim-closed-piece-compatibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lt-closed-pieces)): A continuous Lipschitz map given on a compact parameter manifold by finitely many $C^1$ formulas extending to neighborhoods of their closed pieces has the compatibility in Definition <a href="#lt:compatibility" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lt:compatibility">12</a>. The same assertion applies to compositions of such maps. It continues to hold after a boundary parametrization is changed on each edge by a bi-Lipschitz map whose derivative has an essential limit almost everywhere, and the change is extended by coning.
  - Explanation: Finite closed-piece smooth descriptions supply the essential derivative limits needed for disk parametrization and radial collapse, including the stated composition and edge-change operations.
- **claim-boundary-parametrized-disk** (bound, [source](#stmt-lt-disk)): Put $Q=[-1/2,1/2]^2$. Let $h:Q\to\mathbb R^d$ be a bi-Lipschitz embedding having essential tangential compatibility, and put $$A(h)=\int_Q J_2Dh,\qquad L(h)=\int_{\partial Q}\|D_t h\|^2.$$ For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is a bi-Lipschitz self-homeomorphism $\rho:Q\to Q$, equal to the identity on $\partial Q$, such that $$\int_Q\|D(h\circ\rho)\|^2 \leq C\bigl(A(h)+L(h)\bigr)+\varepsilon, \label{lt:disk-weak}$$ where $C$ is absolute.* *There is also the following sharp form. Suppose $h_j$ are such embeddings, $\sup_jL(h_j)<\infty$, $A(h_j)\leq1+o(1)$, and $h_j\|_{\partial Q}\to\iota\|_{\partial Q}$ uniformly, where $\iota(x)=(x,0)\in\mathbb R^d$. The parametrizations can be chosen so that $$\int_Q\|D(h_j\circ\rho_j)\|^2\leq2+o(1), \qquad D(h_j\circ\rho_j)\longrightarrow D\iota \quad\hbox{in }L^2(Q). \label{lt:disk-sharp}$$ The bi-Lipschitz constants of the chosen parametrizations may depend on the individual embedding. The conclusions transport to polygons through fixed shape-controlled, bi-Lipschitz piecewise smooth charts; the sharp square normalization is used when the constant $2$ matters.
  - Explanation: A boundary-fixing reparametrization bounds Dirichlet energy using image area and boundary energy. Nearly flat boundary values and nearly minimal area additionally give the sharp strong derivative conclusion.
- **claim-subcritical-cell-collapse** (bound, [source](#stmt-lt-collapse)): Let $m\in\{2,3\}$ and $1\leq p<m$. Let $D\subset\mathbb R^m$ be a convex polyhedral cell of scale $\ell$, with inradius bounded below by $c\ell$ and diameter bounded above by $C\ell$. Suppose $H:D\to\mathbb R^d$ is a bi-Lipschitz embedding with essential tangential compatibility. Let $\sigma:\partial D\to\partial D$ be a face-preserving, cellwise bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism. For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is a bi-Lipschitz parametrization $F:D\to H(D)$ with $F\|_{\partial D}=H\circ\sigma$ and $$\int_D\|DF\|^p \leq C_{m,p,c,C}\ell \int_{\partial D}\|D_t(H\circ\sigma)\|^p+\varepsilon. \label{lt:collapse-estimate}$$ The boundary data on shared cells are retained exactly, so the parametrizations glue when those data agree.
  - Explanation: A radial bi-Lipschitz reparametrization preserves the cell image and exact boundary data while controlling interior energy by scaled boundary energy. The strict subcritical inequality makes the radial integral finite.
- **claim-clean-isolated-crossings** (construction, [source](#stmt-lt-clean-crossing)): Let $P$ be a locally flat embedded surface disk and let $I$ be an interval which is straight in specified local topological coordinates. Suppose that $z\in P\cap I$ is interior to both objects, is an isolated point of their intersection, and has a neighborhood containing no other surface sheet under consideration. There is an ambient modification of $P$, supported in an arbitrarily small neighborhood of $z$, after which the intersection in that neighborhood is either empty or consists of a single standard transverse crossing. At a retained crossing the disk can be taken flat across the straight interval. No initial local flatness of the pair $(P,I)$ is assumed.* *If the interval passes from one local side of $P$ to the other at $z$, then a sufficiently small modification retains one crossing.
  - Explanation: A local ambient modification removes an isolated crossing or makes it standard transverse. Passage between opposite local sides forces a crossing to remain.
- **claim-affine-crossing-germs** (construction, [source](#stmt-lt-affine-crossing)): Let $h$ be a homeomorphism between open three-manifolds. A crossing of a regular source surface with the preimage of a target line can be cleaned by applying Lemma <a href="#lt:clean-crossing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lt:clean-crossing">16</a> to the image surface. A crossing of a regular source curve with the preimage of a target surface can be cleaned by applying the Lemma to $h^{-1}$. If the regular strata are straightened in $C^1$ coordinates, the modified homeomorphism can additionally be made affine on a smaller neighborhood of every retained crossing. The only crossings there are the retained transverse ones. Supports can be prescribed to be small in both the source and target.
  - Explanation: Cleaning can be performed in the forward or inverse picture. Straightened regular strata then allow exact affine germs with supports small in both spaces.
- **claim-unknot-two-point-cut** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lt-unknot-cut)): Let $K$ be a tame unknot in the three-sphere and let $B$ be a locally flat ball whose boundary meets $K$ in exactly two standard transverse crossings. Then the proper arc $K\cap B$ is boundary-parallel in $B$. In particular, the ball–arc pair is the standard unknotted interval pair.
  - Explanation: Cutting a tame unknot by a locally flat ball with the specified transverse boundary intersections produces a boundary-parallel proper arc.
- **claim-marked-radial-shell-product** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lt-ray-product)): Let $0<a<b$, let $v_1,\ldots,v_n\in S^2$ be distinct, with $n\geq3$, and put $$M=\{x\in\mathbb R^3:a\leq \|x\|\leq b\},\qquad L_i=\{t v_i:a\leq t\leq b\}.$$ Suppose that $S\subset\operatorname{int}M$ is a locally flat embedded sphere separating the two boundary spheres of $M$. Suppose that $S$ meets each $L_i$ at exactly one point $z_i$, and that these intersections are standard topological transverse crossings: the surface–arc pair has a local chart onto a plane and a transverse straight line. Let $U$ be the closed region between $\{\|x\|=a\}$ and $S$. Then there is a homeomorphism of pairs $$\Phi:\bigl(S^2\times[0,1],\{v_1,\ldots,v_n\}\times[0,1]\bigr) \longrightarrow \bigl(U,\textstyle\bigcup_i(L_i\cap U)\bigr)$$ with $\Phi(q,0)=a q$ and $\Phi(v_i,1)=z_i$. Moreover, for any such product parametrization, the upper boundary map $\phi(q)=\Phi(q,1)$ satisfies $$q\longmapsto \frac{\phi(q)}{\|\phi(q)\|}\simeq\operatorname{id}_{S^2} \quad\text{relative to }\{v_1,\ldots,v_n\}.$$ This homotopy takes unmarked points to unmarked points at every time. Equivalently, the link identification supplied by the product agrees, up to a homotopy preserving the marked points and their complement, with radial projection.
  - Explanation: The region between the inner sphere and a separating sphere is a product respecting the labelled radial strands. Its boundary identification agrees with radial projection up to the prescribed marked homotopy.
- **claim-star-center-identity-germ** (construction, [source](#stmt-lt-star)): Use centered Euclidean coordinates in the source and target of a local homeomorphism $h$, with $h(0)=0$. Let $$D=\{v_1,\ldots,v_n\}\subset S^2,\qquad n\geq3, \qquad R_i=\{t v_i:t\geq0\},$$ be distinct labelled directions, partitioned into forward and reverse sets $F$ and $E$. Each nonempty set has at least two elements. Choose narrow round cones $C_i$ about $R_i$, and slightly wider round cones $C_i^+$ with pairwise disjoint angular closures. Assume, as germs at the origin, that $$h(R_i)\subset\operatorname{int}C_i\quad(i\in F), \qquad h^{-1}(R_i)\subset\operatorname{int}C_i\quad(i\in E).$$ For each nonempty distorted system, assume there are nested round spheres with radii tending to zero in its own space, each meeting every arc of that system exactly once, with the arc passing from one side of the sphere to the other. Impose one of the following angular tests.* 1. *There is a finite based generating loop system $(\gamma_1,\ldots,\gamma_{n-1})$ for $S^2\setminus D$, chosen outside the closed angular caps of all the cones. At arbitrarily small common source radii $r$, all parametrized loops $$s\longmapsto \frac{h(r\gamma_a(s))}{\|h(r\gamma_a(s))\|}$$ are close to $\gamma_a$ within a fixed simultaneous homotopy tolerance in $S^2\setminus D$. The tolerance also keeps the image loops outside the cone caps. Their common basepoint is identified with the original basepoint through the small basepoint neighborhood in this tolerance.* 2. *There are only forward directions, all lying cyclically on a source equator and corresponding to the same target equator directions. The map $h$ preserves orientation. At arbitrarily small source radii the parametrized radial projection of the image of that equatorial circle is uniformly close to its original parametrization, with tolerance small compared with the separations of successive marked points.* *The cones are taken narrow enough relative to these tolerances and separations. Then $h$ can be changed in arbitrarily small paired neighborhoods of the center to equal $\operatorname{id}$ as a germ. The forward and reverse angular conditions persist, with the margins from $C_i$ to $C_i^+$ if necessary. Any additional strict safety conditions away from the germ are preserved by making the supports sufficiently small. The assertion imposes no identity condition at other points of the original intervals.
  - Explanation: Separate angular sectors, single-crossing sections, and a marked angular test permit replacement by an identity germ. The result preserves strict safety margins without imposing identity along the remaining original intervals.
- **claim-relative-regularization-germs** (construction, [source](#stmt-lt-relative-germs)): Suppose a homeomorphism has been made affine in specified $C^1$ coordinates near a disjoint locally finite family of crossing points, and has prescribed exact affine or identity germs at finitely many star centers. It admits a fine approximation by a locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism retaining smaller exact germs. On each compact the approximation is given by finitely many $C^1$ formulas on closed pieces. Positive-gap avoidance conditions, the retained transverse crossing counts, and strict angular conditions can be preserved. Fine approximation tolerances can also impose reciprocal closeness on compact sets.
  - Explanation: Relative PL approximation and supported coordinate changes give a locally bi-Lipschitz replacement retaining smaller exact germs, transverse counts, angular margins, and reciprocal compact closeness.
- **claim-generic-trace-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lt-trace-slicing)): Let $f:\Omega\to\Omega'$ be a homeomorphism of open subsets of $\mathbb R^3$ with $f\in W^{1,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}$, $1\leq p\leq2$. Consider locally finite families of compact Lipschitz semialgebraic parametrizations of dimension $m=1$, and also of dimension $m=2$ if $p=2$. Almost every translation of each template can be chosen so that its $f$-image is countably $m$-rectifiable and has the area formula with tangential derivative, counted with the parametrization’s multiplicity. The same conclusions hold on all lower strata needed in a finite stratification. At a generic intersection with a complementary-dimensional affine test stratum, the source trace has a regular manifold stratum and regular full-rank parameter branches, with locally constant multiplicities. Exceptional image values, including branch junctions and dimension-drop intersections, may be excluded from such intersections.
  - Explanation: Generic translated templates have rectifiable images with the multiplicity area formula. Complementary affine tests avoid exceptional image values and meet regular source branches with locally constant multiplicities.
- **claim-target-lattice-meshes** (construction, [source](#stmt-lt-target-mesh)): In an open subset $V\subset\mathbb R^3$, prescribe finitely many separated compact patches with buffers. In a deep part of each patch one may require a rotated rectangular lattice of side lengths $d$ or $\eta d$, where $0<\eta\leq1$ is fixed. There are locally finite triangulations of $V$ agreeing there with the chain, or Kuhn, triangulation of that lattice and with arbitrarily small prescribed local upper size bounds. For each local candidate, its sampling variables admit a physical common translation $t$ and normalized relative coordinates $z$ whose *joint* density satisfies $$q(t,z)\leq Cd^{-3},\qquad t\in B(t_0,Cd),\quad z\in Z\subset B(0,C), \label{lt:mesh-joint-density}$$ where the dimension and measure of $Z$ are bounded. Deterministic lattice relative coordinates are held fixed; when there are no free relative coordinates their integration means a unit point mass. The bounds are universal away from enlarged lattice patches and may depend on $\eta$ inside them. Only boundedly many candidates occur within a bounded number of local mesh lengths. Formula <a href="#lt:mesh-joint-density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="lt:mesh-joint-density">[lt:mesh-joint-density]</a> is used by joint integration, without normalizing a conditional translation law after fixing $z$.
  - Explanation: Target triangulations retain buffered rectangular lattice patches and admit a bounded joint sampling density. Estimates use the unnormalized joint law rather than a conditional translation distribution.
- **claim-compatible-simplex-squeeze** (construction, [source](#stmt-lt-squeeze)): Let $\mathcal T$ be a locally finite triangulation of a three-dimensional open domain. Assign constants $0<b_\sigma\leq1$ to its tetrahedra, and to every nonempty face $\rho$ assign the minimum of the constants of its incident tetrahedra. On each tetrahedron put $$\begin{aligned} b(\lambda)&= \min_{\varnothing\ne\rho\subseteq\sigma} \left(b_\rho+ \frac{\max_{i\notin\rho}\lambda_i}{\max_j\lambda_j}\right), \label{lt:b-definition}\\ a(\lambda)&=e^{1/b(\lambda)}-e, \qquad T_i(\lambda)= \frac{\lambda_i e^{a(\lambda)\lambda_i}} {\sum_j\lambda_j e^{a(\lambda)\lambda_j}}, \label{lt:squeeze-definition} \end{aligned}$$ where the maximum over an empty set in <a href="#lt:b-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="lt:b-definition">[lt:b-definition]</a> is zero. These formulas define a face-preserving, locally bi-Lipschitz self-homeomorphism $T$ of the domain, with finitely many $C^1$ formulas on closed pieces of every compact subset. The normalized Lipschitz constant of $b$ is bounded absolutely, independently of its minimum, and a.e. $$\|Da\|\leq C(a+1)(1+\log(a+1))^2. \label{lt:a-derivative}$$ If all the incident constants in a mesh neighborhood are one, $T$ is the identity on its inner simplices.* *Let $G$ parametrize finitely many compact $m$-traces, $m\in\{1,2\}$, by finite closed-piece $C^1$ formulas, and suppose their intersections with the $Z_{\sigma,I}$ are regular transverse hits away from higher ties and simplex boundaries. Every counted hit is in the interior of each counted regular $m$-dimensional parameter branch; parameter boundaries and lower-dimensional parameter strata avoid the counted dual strata. As the lower bounds of $a$ tend to infinity on the measured neighborhood, $$\int J_mD(T\circ G) \longrightarrow \sum_{\text{hits on }Z_{\sigma,I}} \mathcal H^m(\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\{v_i:i\in I\}), \label{lt:squeeze-area}$$ with multiplicity. The local convergence, and in particular its upper bound with arbitrarily small error, is uniform when other floors of $a$ are made arbitrarily larger. Continuous weights may be bounded by their simplex suprema in this conclusion.
  - Explanation: Compatible barycentric exponential formulas define a locally bi-Lipschitz target homeomorphism. For regular finite trace data, increasing the squeezing floors converts trace measurements into the multiplicity-weighted flat face budgets.
- **claim-transfer-tie-budgets** (construction, [source](#stmt-lt-budget-transfer)): Fix generic trace data as in Lemma <a href="#lt:trace-slicing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lt:trace-slicing">22</a> and a sufficiently fine generic mesh from Lemma <a href="#lt:target-mesh" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lt:target-mesh">23</a>. There is an onto locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism $H_0:\Omega\to\Omega'$, locally specified by finitely many $C^1$ formulas on closed pieces, whose image lengths and areas on the measured traces are at most their tie budgets plus arbitrarily small prescribed local errors. Nonnegative continuous output weights are allowed, with the simplex-supremum budgets of Definition <a href="#lt:tie-budget" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lt:tie-budget">24</a>.* *The map can be arbitrarily close to $f$, with reciprocal closeness on interior compact sets, within the mesh displacement and chosen local modification errors. It can retain smaller exact affine germs previously installed at a locally finite family of crossings and finitely many star centers, and can retain strict compact forward and inverse safety conditions, including angular cone conditions, whenever they have positive margins. For this relative usage, measured data are separated from buffered open exemptions around the protected germs: a measured trace either avoids the exempt cores, or its weight vanishes there with a buffer. Every germ required to remain exact in $H_0$ is contained in one of these buffered exemptions. The affine crossing germs installed at the newly cleaned measured hits are retained only in the intermediate map $G$ for the concentration calculation; no affine-germ condition at those hits is imposed on $T\circ G$. The mesh and all measured configurations are fixed before the squeezing floors are selected.
  - Explanation: Cleaning, relative regularization, and target squeezing produce an onto locally bi-Lipschitz map whose measured traces obey the original budgets. Exact protected germs require buffered exemptions from the measurements.
- **claim-expected-tie-budgets** (bound, [source](#stmt-lt-budget-expectation)): For an $m$-trace with finite image measure, the meshes in Lemma <a href="#lt:target-mesh" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lt:target-mesh">23</a> satisfy $$\mathbb E\,\mathcal B_m(\Gamma) \leq C\int_K J_mD(f\circ P), \label{lt:expected-budget}$$ with parameter multiplicity understood. The constant is universal outside enlarged lattice patches and may depend on their fixed aspect ratios inside them. The estimate holds locally with comparable enlargements. For continuous weights it holds with the weighted trace integral and an arbitrarily small enlargement error as the local mesh sizes tend to zero. Fixed linear output normalizations may be included in all areas.
  - Explanation: Joint translation coarea bounds expected weighted crossing costs by image trace measure. Constants are universal outside enlarged lattice patches and may depend on aspect ratio inside.
- **claim-high-probability-curve-budgets** (bound, [source](#stmt-lt-curve-probability)): For any compact rectifiable measured curve $\Gamma$ of finite length, any $\delta>0$, and any $e>0$, sufficiently small local mesh upper bounds make $$\mathcal B_1(\Gamma)\leq C\operatorname{length}(\Gamma)+e \label{lt:curve-high-probability}$$ hold with probability at least $1-\delta$. The constant $C$ is independent of $\delta,e$ and the required mesh resolution; it is universal away from enlarged patches. Prescribed summable failure probabilities and local errors can be imposed simultaneously on a countable locally finite family of curves. In a deep anisotropic lattice patch there is also a grid-unit version: rescale to unit grid aspect, estimate length there, and convert the weight back using the longest physical side, with a constant independent of the aspect ratio in those units.
  - Explanation: Fine enough meshes give a length budget with arbitrarily high probability while keeping the length coefficient independent of the failure allowance. Summable allowances handle locally finite curve families.
- **claim-sharp-thin-grid-costs** (bound, [source](#stmt-lt-sharp-grid)): In a deep lattice patch adapted to a fixed $k$-plane $E$, use side $d$ along $E$ and side $\eta d$ along $E^\perp$, where $k\in\{1,2\}$. For $m=k$, the translation-averaged tie-budget coefficient on a simple $k$-vector tangent to $E$ is at most $1+C\eta$ times its Euclidean norm. For general measured $m$-vectors, tangent contributions have constants uniform in $\eta$, and errors have a finite constant $C_\eta$. After a fixed linear output normalization $A$ which keeps the two subspaces orthogonal, the sharp coefficient relative to the normalized tangent length or area is at most $1+C_A\eta$. Here $C_A$ depends only on $\\|A\\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ and $\\|A^{-1}\\|_{\mathrm{op}}$. The tangent bounds remain uniform in $\eta$, while the error constants may also depend on these two norms. The grid-unit bound of Lemma <a href="#lt:curve-probability" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lt:curve-probability">28</a> also has no aspect-dependent factor on tangent lengths.
  - Explanation: Thin-grid averaging makes tangent costs nearly Euclidean. Tangent bounds remain uniform as the grid thins, whereas errors can carry aspect-dependent constants.
- **claim-shape-controlled-source-sampling** (construction, [source](#stmt-lt-source-mesh)): There are locally finite source meshes of uniform shape with arbitrarily small local upper size bounds, preserving finitely many separated deep cubical lattice patches in prescribed orientations and with prescribed common fine scales. Deep cells may remain cubes; elsewhere they are tetrahedra, with matching facet diagonals at interfaces. For an integrable nonnegative function $g$ and $m\in\{1,2\}$, their sampled $m$-faces satisfy the local estimate $$\mathbb E\sum_F \ell_F^{3-m}\int_F g\,d\mathcal H^m \leq C\int g, \label{lt:source-fubini}$$ with comparable local enlargements and bounded overlap understood. In particular this applies to $g=\|Df\|^p$ and controls the tangential derivative trace integrals. Lattice shifts can also be uniform over any prescribed macro-period formed by grouping fine cubes.
  - Explanation: Source meshes retain uniform shapes and prescribed cubical regions. Averaged face traces, weighted by their scales, are bounded by the ambient integral.
- **claim-low-exponent-bilip-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-la-approximation)): Let $\Omega,\Omega'\subset\mathbb R^3$ be bounded domains, let $1\le p\le2$, and let $f:\Omega\to\Omega'$ be a homeomorphism in $W^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb R^3)$. For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is a locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism $H:\Omega\to\Omega'$ such that $$\label{la:goal} \int_\Omega \bigl(\|H-f\|^p+\|DH-Df\|^p\bigr)<\varepsilon.$$ In particular, the target of $H$ is exactly $\Omega'$, and $H\in W^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb R^3)$.
  - Explanation: Rank-sensitive cores, trace budgets, and compatible cell completion give strong locally bi-Lipschitz approximation with global Sobolev membership and exactly the original target.
- **claim-tangential-composition-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-la-composition-limit)): Let $D$ be a compact Lipschitz parameter piece of dimension $d$, and let $q_j,q_0:D\to U$ be Lipschitz maps into an open subset of a Euclidean space. Suppose that their images lie in one compact subset of $U$, that $q_j\to q_0$ uniformly, and that $$Dq_j\longrightarrow Dq_0\quad\hbox{a.e. on }D, \qquad \sup_j\\|Dq_j\\|_{L^\infty(D)}<\infty.$$ Let $v:U\to\mathbb R^a$ be Lipschitz on a neighborhood of that compact set. Suppose there are finitely many closed pieces $C_\nu$ covering the neighborhood and $C^1$ maps $v_\nu$, defined on neighborhoods of $C_\nu$, such that $v=v_\nu$ on $C_\nu$. Then, for every finite $r\ge1$, $$\label{la:composition-convergence} D(v\circ q_j)\longrightarrow D(v\circ q_0) \quad\hbox{in }L^r(D).$$ No rank assumption is imposed on $Dq_0$. The assertion applies separately to an edge, a face, or a volume piece.
  - Explanation: Finite closed-piece smooth formulas permit strong convergence of composed derivatives even when the limiting parameter map loses rank. The proof uses locality on the parameter domain.
- **claim-radial-controlled-blocks** (construction, [source](#stmt-la-radial-blocks)): Fix finitely many separated compact configurations satisfying <a href="#la:identity-jets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="la:identity-jets">[la:identity-jets]</a>, and fix $l$. For sufficiently small $h$ one can retain candidate blocks with the following properties.* 1. *The expected $m$-volume of discarded candidate blocks tends to zero as $h\to0$. The union of retained blocks therefore differs from $K$ by a set whose expected measure tends to zero.* 2. *Each retained $Q$ has the core $$B_Q=a+(1-8\gamma)(Q-a).$$ The complement consists of shape-controlled frusta of scale $lh$, one over each boundary tile. After the common budget construction and the source and target changes described below, the map on $B_Q$ is $b$, except on caps of total measure at most $C\gamma \|Q\|$. Its tangential derivative on the whole core and its core boundary is bounded by $C_b$.* 3. *Every radial connector in the final shell has image length at most $C_b\gamma h$. Nonincident mesh edges avoid the radial-angular expansions. Subsequent cap contractions multiply their length budgets by at most a fixed constant. With $\ell=lh$, the converted shell edge cost after constant-speed parametrization is $\ell^{m-p}\sum_e L_e^p$, where $L_e$ is the image length. Its bound is $O_b(l)\sum_Q\|Q\|+o(1)$ after the source selection and individual curve-budget bounds, with arbitrary additional transfer errors.* 4. *If $m=3,p=2$, include the full wedge from $a$ to each boundary tile edge among the measured surfaces. There are bounded continuous weights, fixed before the target budget mesh, for which the final connector-face areas are bounded by the weighted $H_0$-areas, up to arbitrarily small errors. After multiplication by $lh$, the expectation of their total converted budget $\mathcal B_{\mathrm{wedge}}$ satisfies $$\label{la:wedge-cost} \mathbb E\mathcal B_{\mathrm{wedge}} \le C_b\gamma\sum_i \|K_i\|+o(1).$$ Outer ordinary edges and faces have converted cost $O_b(l)\sum_Q\|Q\|+o(1)$ on these configurations.* *The construction can be performed simultaneously with the other budgeted pieces used below. In a volume block the only measured interior surfaces are its wedges. In the plane-section application, any additional graph to be fixed is assumed to have positive clearance from $K$ and its image. Taking $h$ smaller then keeps all expansion and cap supports off that graph. More precisely, write $V$ for the ambient initial map after its output motions and $q_Q:Q\to Q$ for the radial parameter change. The controlled trace is $V\circ q_Q$. For $m=3$ these parameter changes are ambient source self-homeomorphisms; for $m=2$ they are retained solely as section parametrizations. The graph preservation means that the radial motions leave its already-budgeted $H_0$-image unchanged; it does not assert equality of $H_0$ with $f$ on that graph.
  - Explanation: Selected blocks expand exact germs into controlled cores, reduce shell spoke lengths, and bound critical wedge costs using weights fixed before transfer. Later attenuation parameters use the actual finite configuration.
- **claim-finite-compact-jets** (construction, [source](#stmt-la-jet-selection)): Given $\varepsilon_0>0$, one can choose $M<\infty$ and finitely many pairwise disjoint compact sets $K_i\Subset\Omega$, of ranks $k_i\in\{1,2,3\}$, such that $$\label{la:jet-tail} \int_{\Omega\setminus\bigcup_iK_i}\|Df\|^p<\varepsilon_0.$$ On their nonzero singular values there are common bounds $M^{-1}$ and $M$. At rank three the sets have ambient $C^1$ diffeomorphism charts $b_i$ agreeing with the values and derivatives of $f$, with chart bounds $C_M$. At ranks one and two, for any subsequently chosen $\delta>0$, the pieces can be refined so that $$\label{la:rank-model} \|Df-D_i\|\le\delta\quad\hbox{on }K_i, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{rank}}D_i=k_i,$$ with the same type of singular-value bounds. The compact pieces can be enclosed in disjoint source neighborhoods and corresponding disjoint target neighborhoods with buffers. Once all finite multiplicative constants are fixed, the neighborhoods can be chosen so that $$\label{la:excess-neighborhood} \sum_i\int_{U_i\setminus K_i}(1+\|Df\|^p)$$ is as small as prescribed, and the inverse images of the buffered target neighborhoods lie in the corresponding $U_i$.
  - Explanation: Compact positive-rank jet sets carry all but a small derivative-energy tail. Full-rank jets extend to ambient diffeomorphism charts; singular jets are refined near fixed rank models before excess neighborhoods are reduced.
- **claim-successful-kernel-blocks** (bound, [source](#stmt-la-kernel-selection)): For fixed $M,l,\tau$, the total expected volume of candidate rank-one or rank-two blocks failing these trace tests tends to zero as first $\delta\to0$ and then $h\to0$, with sufficiently small excess neighborhoods. In successful blocks the transferred unparametrized shell data have converted cost $$\label{la:kernel-shell-budget} O_M(l)\sum_Q\|Q\|+\text{an arbitrarily small error}.$$ Here converted edge cost means $(lh)^{3-p}$ times image length to the power $p$, and, when $p=2$, converted face cost means $lh$ times image area. Fixed factors depending on $l$ and $\eta$ are allowed on the trace errors.
  - Explanation: Translation averaging and trace tests make failed kernel-block volume small. Successful shell measurements have small converted cost, with fixed inflation factors charged only to trace errors.
- **claim-uncontrolled-cell-completion** (construction, [source](#stmt-la-cell-completion)): Let $D$ be an uncontrolled source three-cell of scale $H$ in the construction. Its base embedding $v:D\to\mathbb R^3$ is bi-Lipschitz and is given by finitely many $C^1$ formulas extending to neighborhoods of their closed pieces. Write $g$ for the prescribed or subsequently chosen boundary data. On every prescribed core face $F$, assume that $g\|_F=v\|_F\circ s_F$, where $s_F:F\to F$ is a bi-Lipschitz self-homeomorphism preserving each edge. These self-maps agree on shared edges. When $p=2$, their edge restrictions have derivatives with essential limits almost everywhere. Then the cell can be reparametrized bi-Lipschitzly, preserving its base image and all prescribed boundary data, with derivative cost at most a constant times $$\label{la:cell-cost} \begin{split} H\sum_{F\text{ prescribed}}\int_F\|D_tg\|^p &+H^2\sum_{e\subset\partial D}\int_e\|D_tg\|^p\\ &+\mathbf1_{\{p=2\}}\, H\sum_{F\text{ uncontrolled}}\operatorname{Area}(v\|_F) \end{split}$$ plus any prescribed positive error. An unprescribed edge of length comparable to $H$ may be given energy at most $C\operatorname{length}(v(e))^pH^{1-p}$. The constants depend only on $p$ and the fixed shape bounds, and adjacent cells can be completed with identical shared-face data.
  - Explanation: Common edge choices, disk or planar collapse estimates, and volume collapse complete uncontrolled cells while preserving each base image and all prescribed shared-face data.

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-sm-proper-degree",
          "exact_quote": "Let $h:\\Omega\\to\\Omega'$ be an orientation-preserving homeomorphism between bounded domains, and let $g:\\Omega\\to\\mathbb R^3$ be a smooth map with $\\det Dg>0$.           "source_id": "PM-368.1"
        }

      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sm-smoothing",
          "exact_quote": "For every $\\varepsilon>0$ and every continuous function $\\xi:\\Omega\\to(0,\\infty)$, there is a $C^\\infty$ diffeomorphism $g:\\Omega\\to\\Omega'$ such that $$\\label{sm:smoothing-conclusion}\n \\left\\lVert g-H\\right\\rVert_{W^{1,p}(\\Omega)}<\\varepsilon,\n \\qquad |g(x)-H(x)|<\\xi(x)\\quad(x\\in\\Omega).$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sm-mesh",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute integer $n_0$ with the following property. Given arbitrary positive local upper bounds on the fine scale, there is a locally finite dyadic cube decomposition $\\mathcal Q$ of $\\Omega$ with centers $c_Q$ and side lengths $l_Q$ for which, on writing $$\\label{sm:patch-box}\n B_Q(s)=c_Q+[-s l_Q,s l_Q]^3,$$ the following hold:*\n\n1.  *$B_Q(100)\\Subset\\Omega$, and cubes meeting $B_Q(50)$ have side lengths comparable to $l_Q$ by absolute constants.*\n\n2.  *The normalized cube configurations in $B_Q(6)$ belong to a finite list. There is a conforming affine triangulation of $\\mathcal Q$ with finitely many normalized nondegenerate shapes on these configurations.*\n\n3.  *The cubes have $n_0$ colors so that the closed boxes $B_Q(6)$ of a single color are pairwise disjoint.*\n\n4.  *Attach to $Q$ a coarse index $P(Q)$ containing $c_Q$, using a fixed convention on coarse boundaries. The initial scale bounds can be reduced so that all interactions through any fixed number of neighboring patch layers are confined to a fixed locally finite graph of coarse indices, independent of further mesh refinement. The corresponding patches lie in the neighborhoods on which the required bounds in Equation <a href=\"#sm:coarse-bilip\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sm:coarse-bilip\">[sm:coarse-bilip]</a> hold.*\n\n*The upper bounds on the fine scale remain arbitrarily prescribable.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-sm-insertion",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a patch $Q$ and a finite collection of older protected boxes with the relative sizes and positions provided by Lemma <a href=\"#sm:mesh\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sm:mesh\">9</a>. For every older box prescribe a larger half-shrunk box and a smaller fully-shrunk box, separated by a fixed positive normalized margin. Let $G:\\Omega\\to\\Omega'$ be a homeomorphism, and let $h_Q$ be a PL embedding on a neighborhood of $B_Q(3)$. Assume that $h_Q=G$ on the intersection of $B_Q(3)$ with each half-shrunk box.*\n\n\n*For every $\\lambda>0$ there is an $a>0$, depending only on $\\lambda$, the normalized source configuration, and a local constant $K_P$ in Equation <a href=\"#sm:coarse-bilip\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sm:coarse-bilip\">[sm:coarse-bilip]</a>, such that $$\\label{sm:insertion-smallness}\n \\sup_{B_Q(5)}|G-H|<a l_Q,\n \\qquad \\sup_{B_Q(3)}|h_Q-H|<a l_Q$$ implies the following conclusion. There is a domain homeomorphism $A$ supported in $B_Q(5)$, with $$\\sup|A-\\operatorname{id}|<\\lambda l_Q,$$ such that $G\\circ A=h_Q$ on a neighborhood of $B_Q(2)$ and $G\\circ A=G$ on the fully-shrunk older boxes. The tolerances are uniform over a finite list of source configurations. They do not depend on the derivatives of $G$ or of $h_Q$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-closed-pieces",
        "exact_quote": "A continuous Lipschitz map given on a compact parameter manifold by finitely many $C^1$ formulas extending to neighborhoods of their closed pieces has the compatibility in Definition <a href=\"#lt:compatibility\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lt:compatibility\">12</a>. The same assertion applies to compositions of such maps. It continues to hold after a boundary parametrization is changed on each edge by a bi-Lipschitz map whose derivative has an essential limit almost everywhere, and the change is extended by coning.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "A continuous Lipschitz map given on a compact parameter manifold by finitely many $C^1$ formulas extending to neighborhoods of their closed pieces has the compatibility in Definition <a href=\"#lt:compatibility\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lt:compatibility\">12</a>. The same assertion applies to compositions of such maps. It continues to hold after a boundary parametrization is changed on each edge by a bi-Lipschitz map whose derivative has an essential limit almost everywhere, and the change is extended by coning.",
          "source_id": "PM-368.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-disk",
        "exact_quote": "Put $Q=[-1/2,1/2]^2$. Let $h:Q\\to\\mathbb R^d$ be a bi-Lipschitz embedding having essential tangential compatibility, and put $$A(h)=\\int_Q J_2Dh,\\qquad\n L(h)=\\int_{\\partial Q}|D_t h|^2.$$ For every $\\varepsilon>0$ there is a bi-Lipschitz self-homeomorphism $\\rho:Q\\to Q$, equal to the identity on $\\partial Q$, such that $$\\int_Q|D(h\\circ\\rho)|^2\n       \\leq C\\bigl(A(h)+L(h)\\bigr)+\\varepsilon,\n \\label{lt:disk-weak}$$ where $C$ is absolute.*\n\n\n*There is also the following sharp form. Suppose $h_j$ are such embeddings, $\\sup_jL(h_j)<\\infty$, $A(h_j)\\leq1+o(1)$, and $h_j|_{\\partial Q}\\to\\iota|_{\\partial Q}$ uniformly, where $\\iota(x)=(x,0)\\in\\mathbb R^d$. The parametrizations can be chosen so that $$\\int_Q|D(h_j\\circ\\rho_j)|^2\\leq2+o(1),\n \\qquad\n D(h_j\\circ\\rho_j)\\longrightarrow D\\iota\n       \\quad\\hbox{in }L^2(Q).\n \\label{lt:disk-sharp}$$ The bi-Lipschitz constants of the chosen parametrizations may depend on the individual embedding. The conclusions transport to polygons through fixed shape-controlled, bi-Lipschitz piecewise smooth charts; the sharp square normalization is used when the constant $2$ matters.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Suppose $h_j$ are such embeddings, $\\sup_jL(h_j)<\\infty$, $A(h_j)\\leq1+o(1)$, and $h_j|_{\\partial Q}\\to\\iota|_{\\partial Q}$ uniformly, where $\\iota(x)=(x,0)\\in\\mathbb R^d$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-collapse",
        "exact_quote": "Let $m\\in\\{2,3\\}$ and $1\\leq p<m$. Let $D\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ be a convex polyhedral cell of scale $\\ell$, with inradius bounded below by $c\\ell$ and diameter bounded above by $C\\ell$. Suppose $H:D\\to\\mathbb R^d$ is a bi-Lipschitz embedding with essential tangential compatibility. Let $\\sigma:\\partial D\\to\\partial D$ be a face-preserving, cellwise bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism. For every $\\varepsilon>0$ there is a bi-Lipschitz parametrization $F:D\\to H(D)$ with $F|_{\\partial D}=H\\circ\\sigma$ and $$\\int_D|DF|^p\n       \\leq C_{m,p,c,C}\\ell\n                    \\int_{\\partial D}|D_t(H\\circ\\sigma)|^p+\\varepsilon.\n \\label{lt:collapse-estimate}$$ The boundary data on shared cells are retained exactly, so the parametrizations glue when those data agree.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-clean-crossing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a locally flat embedded surface disk and let $I$ be an interval which is straight in specified local topological coordinates. Suppose that $z\\in P\\cap I$ is interior to both objects, is an isolated point of their intersection, and has a neighborhood containing no other surface sheet under consideration. There is an ambient modification of $P$, supported in an arbitrarily small neighborhood of $z$, after which the intersection in that neighborhood is either empty or consists of a single standard transverse crossing. At a retained crossing the disk can be taken flat across the straight interval. No initial local flatness of the pair $(P,I)$ is assumed.*\n\n*If the interval passes from one local side of $P$ to the other at $z$, then a sufficiently small modification retains one crossing.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a locally flat embedded surface disk and let $I$ be an interval which is straight in specified local topological coordinates. At a retained crossing the disk can be taken flat across the straight interval.         }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-affine-crossing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h$ be a homeomorphism between open three-manifolds. A crossing of a regular source surface with the preimage of a target line can be cleaned by applying Lemma <a href=\"#lt:clean-crossing\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lt:clean-crossing\">16</a> to the image surface. A crossing of a regular source curve with the preimage of a target surface can be cleaned by applying the Lemma to $h^{-1}$. If the regular strata are straightened in $C^1$ coordinates, the modified homeomorphism can additionally be made affine on a smaller neighborhood of every retained crossing. The only crossings there are the retained transverse ones. Supports can be prescribed to be small in both the source and target.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-ray-product",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lt:punctured-product\" label=\"lt:punctured-product\"></span>Let $0<a<b$, let $v_1,\\ldots,v_n\\in S^2$ be distinct, with $n\\geq3$, and put $$M=\\{x\\in\\mathbb R^3:a\\leq |x|\\leq b\\},\\qquad\n L_i=\\{t v_i:a\\leq t\\leq b\\}.$$ Suppose that $S\\subset\\operatorname{int}M$ is a locally flat embedded sphere separating the two boundary spheres of $M$. Suppose that $S$ meets each $L_i$ at exactly one point $z_i$, and that these intersections are standard topological transverse crossings: the surface–arc pair has a local chart onto a plane and a transverse straight line. Let $U$ be the closed region between $\\{|x|=a\\}$ and $S$. Then there is a homeomorphism of pairs $$\\Phi:\\bigl(S^2\\times[0,1],\\{v_1,\\ldots,v_n\\}\\times[0,1]\\bigr)\n \\longrightarrow\n \\bigl(U,\\textstyle\\bigcup_i(L_i\\cap U)\\bigr)$$ with $\\Phi(q,0)=a q$ and $\\Phi(v_i,1)=z_i$. Moreover, for any such product parametrization, the upper boundary map $\\phi(q)=\\Phi(q,1)$ satisfies $$q\\longmapsto \\frac{\\phi(q)}{|\\phi(q)|}\\simeq\\operatorname{id}_{S^2}\n \\quad\\text{relative to }\\{v_1,\\ldots,v_n\\}.$$ This homotopy takes unmarked points to unmarked points at every time. Equivalently, the link identification supplied by the product agrees, up to a homotopy preserving the marked points and their complement, with radial projection.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-star",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lt:star-replacement\" label=\"lt:star-replacement\"></span>Use centered Euclidean coordinates in the source and target of a local homeomorphism $h$, with $h(0)=0$. Let $$D=\\{v_1,\\ldots,v_n\\}\\subset S^2,\\qquad n\\geq3,\n \\qquad R_i=\\{t v_i:t\\geq0\\},$$ be distinct labelled directions, partitioned into forward and reverse sets $F$ and $E$. Each nonempty set has at least two elements. Choose narrow round cones $C_i$ about $R_i$, and slightly wider round cones $C_i^+$ with pairwise disjoint angular closures. Assume, as germs at the origin, that $$h(R_i)\\subset\\operatorname{int}C_i\\quad(i\\in F),\n \\qquad h^{-1}(R_i)\\subset\\operatorname{int}C_i\\quad(i\\in E).$$ For each nonempty distorted system, assume there are nested round spheres with radii tending to zero in its own space, each meeting every arc of that system exactly once, with the arc passing from one side of the sphere to the other. Impose one of the following angular tests.*\n\n1.  *There is a finite based generating loop system $(\\gamma_1,\\ldots,\\gamma_{n-1})$ for $S^2\\setminus D$, chosen outside the closed angular caps of all the cones. At arbitrarily small common source radii $r$, all parametrized loops $$s\\longmapsto \\frac{h(r\\gamma_a(s))}{|h(r\\gamma_a(s))|}$$ are close to $\\gamma_a$ within a fixed simultaneous homotopy tolerance in $S^2\\setminus D$. The tolerance also keeps the image loops outside the cone caps. Their common basepoint is identified with the original basepoint through the small basepoint neighborhood in this tolerance.*\n\n2.  *There are only forward directions, all lying cyclically on a source equator and corresponding to the same target equator directions. The map $h$ preserves orientation. At arbitrarily small source radii the parametrized radial projection of the image of that equatorial circle is uniformly close to its original parametrization, with tolerance small compared with the separations of successive marked points.*\n\n*The cones are taken narrow enough relative to these tolerances and separations. Then $h$ can be changed in arbitrarily small paired neighborhoods of the center to equal $\\operatorname{id}$ as a germ. The forward and reverse angular conditions persist, with the margins from $C_i$ to $C_i^+$ if necessary. Any additional strict safety conditions away from the germ are preserved by making the supports sufficiently small. The assertion imposes no identity condition at other points of the original intervals.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },

Each nonempty set has at least two elements. Choose narrow round cones $C_i$ about $R_i$, and slightly wider round cones $C_i^+$ with pairwise disjoint angular closures. Assume, as germs at the origin, that $$h(R_i)\\subset\\operatorname{int}C_i\\quad(i\\in F),\n \\qquad h^{-1}(R_i)\\subset\\operatorname{int}C_i\\quad(i\\in E).$$ For each nonempty distorted system, assume there are nested round spheres with radii tending to zero in its own space, each meeting every arc of that system exactly once, with the arc passing from one side of the sphere to the other. The tolerance also keeps the image loops outside the cone caps. Let $$D=\\{v_1,\\ldots,v_n\\}\\subset S^2,\\qquad n\\geq3,\n \\qquad R_i=\\{t v_i:t\\geq0\\},$$ be distinct labelled directions, partitioned into forward and reverse sets $F$ and $E$. Each nonempty set has at least two elements. Choose narrow round cones $C_i$ about $R_i$, and slightly wider round cones $C_i^+$ with pairwise disjoint angular closures. Assume, as germs at the origin, that $$h(R_i)\\subset\\operatorname{int}C_i\\quad(i\\in F),\n \\qquad h^{-1}(R_i)\\subset\\operatorname{int}C_i\\quad(i\\in E).$$ For each nonempty distorted system, assume there are nested round spheres with radii tending to zero in its own space, each meeting every arc of that system exactly once, with the arc passing from one side of the sphere to the other. Impose one of the following angular tests.*\n\n1.  *There is a finite based generating loop system $(\\gamma_1,\\ldots,\\gamma_{n-1})$ for $S^2\\setminus D$, chosen outside the closed angular caps of all the cones. At arbitrarily small common source radii $r$, all parametrized loops $$s\\longmapsto \\frac{h(r\\gamma_a(s))}{|h(r\\gamma_a(s))|}$$ are close to $\\gamma_a$ within a fixed simultaneous homotopy tolerance in $S^2\\setminus D$. The tolerance also keeps the image loops outside the cone caps. Their common basepoint is identified with the original basepoint through the small basepoint neighborhood in this tolerance.*\n\n2.  *There are only forward directions, all lying cyclically on a source equator and corresponding to the same target equator directions. The map $h$ preserves orientation. At arbitrarily small source radii the parametrized radial projection of the image of that equatorial circle is uniformly close to its original parametrization, with tolerance small compared with the separations of successive marked points.*\n\n*The cones are taken narrow enough relative to these tolerances and separations. Then $h$ can be changed in arbitrarily small paired neighborhoods of the center to equal $\\operatorname{id}$ as a germ. The forward and reverse angular conditions persist, with the margins from $C_i$ to $C_i^+$ if necessary. Any additional strict safety conditions away from the germ are preserved by making the supports sufficiently small. The assertion imposes no identity condition at other points of the original intervals.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-relative-germs",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a homeomorphism has been made affine in specified $C^1$ coordinates near a disjoint locally finite family of crossing points, and has prescribed exact affine or identity germs at finitely many star centers. It admits a fine approximation by a locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism retaining smaller exact germs. On each compact the approximation is given by finitely many $C^1$ formulas on closed pieces. Positive-gap avoidance conditions, the retained transverse crossing counts, and strict angular conditions can be preserved. Fine approximation tolerances can also impose reciprocal closeness on compact sets.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-trace-slicing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:\\Omega\\to\\Omega'$ be a homeomorphism of open subsets of $\\mathbb R^3$ with $f\\in W^{1,p}_{\\mathrm{loc}}$, $1\\leq p\\leq2$. Consider locally finite families of compact Lipschitz semialgebraic parametrizations of dimension $m=1$, and also of dimension $m=2$ if $p=2$. Almost every translation of each template can be chosen so that its $f$-image is countably $m$-rectifiable and has the area formula with tangential derivative, counted with the parametrization’s multiplicity. The same conclusions hold on all lower strata needed in a finite stratification. At a generic intersection with a complementary-dimensional affine test stratum, the source trace has a regular manifold stratum and regular full-rank parameter branches, with locally constant multiplicities. Exceptional image values, including branch junctions and dimension-drop intersections, may be excluded from such intersections.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-target-mesh",
        "exact_quote": "In an open subset $V\\subset\\mathbb R^3$, prescribe finitely many separated compact patches with buffers. In a deep part of each patch one may require a rotated rectangular lattice of side lengths $d$ or $\\eta d$, where $0<\\eta\\leq1$ is fixed. There are locally finite triangulations of $V$ agreeing there with the chain, or Kuhn, triangulation of that lattice and with arbitrarily small prescribed local upper size bounds. For each local candidate, its sampling variables admit a physical common translation $t$ and normalized relative coordinates $z$ whose *joint* density satisfies $$q(t,z)\\leq Cd^{-3},\\qquad\n t\\in B(t_0,Cd),\\quad z\\in Z\\subset B(0,C),\n \\label{lt:mesh-joint-density}$$ where the dimension and measure of $Z$ are bounded. Deterministic lattice relative coordinates are held fixed; when there are no free relative coordinates their integration means a unit point mass. The bounds are universal away from enlarged lattice patches and may depend on $\\eta$ inside them. Only boundedly many candidates occur within a bounded number of local mesh lengths. Formula <a href=\"#lt:mesh-joint-density\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"lt:mesh-joint-density\">[lt:mesh-joint-density]</a> is used by joint integration, without normalizing a conditional translation law after fixing $z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lt-target-mesh",
          "exact_quote": "In an open subset $V\\subset\\mathbb R^3$, prescribe finitely many separated compact patches with buffers. There are locally finite triangulations of $V$ agreeing there with the chain, or Kuhn, triangulation of that lattice and with arbitrarily small prescribed local upper size bounds. For each local candidate, its sampling variables admit a physical common translation $t$ and normalized relative coordinates $z$ whose *joint* density satisfies $$q(t,z)\\leq Cd^{-3},\\qquad\n t\\in B(t_0,Cd),\\quad z\\in Z\\subset B(0,C),\n \\label{lt:mesh-joint-density}$$ where the dimension and measure of $Z$ are bounded. Deterministic lattice relative coordinates are held fixed; when there are no free relative coordinates their integration means a unit point mass. The bounds are universal away from enlarged lattice patches and may depend on $\\eta$ inside them. Only boundedly many candidates occur within a bounded number of local mesh lengths. Formula <a href=\"#lt:mesh-joint-density\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"lt:mesh-joint-density\">[lt:mesh-joint-density]</a> is used by joint integration, without normalizing a conditional translation law after fixing $z$.",
          "source_id": "PM-368.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "In an open subset $V\\subset\\mathbb R^3$, prescribe finitely many separated compact patches with buffers.         "anchor": "stmt-lt-squeeze",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal T$ be a locally finite triangulation of a three-dimensional open domain. Assign constants $0<b_\\sigma\\leq1$ to its tetrahedra, and to every nonempty face $\\rho$ assign the minimum of the constants of its incident tetrahedra. On each tetrahedron put $$\\begin{aligned}\n b(\\lambda)&=\n \\min_{\\varnothing\\ne\\rho\\subseteq\\sigma}\n \\left(b_\\rho+\n        \\frac{\\max_{i\\notin\\rho}\\lambda_i}{\\max_j\\lambda_j}\\right),\n       \\label{lt:b-definition}\\\\\n a(\\lambda)&=e^{1/b(\\lambda)}-e,\n \\qquad\n T_i(\\lambda)=\n \\frac{\\lambda_i e^{a(\\lambda)\\lambda_i}}\n             {\\sum_j\\lambda_j e^{a(\\lambda)\\lambda_j}},\n       \\label{lt:squeeze-definition}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where the maximum over an empty set in <a href=\"#lt:b-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"lt:b-definition\">[lt:b-definition]</a> is zero. These formulas define a face-preserving, locally bi-Lipschitz self-homeomorphism $T$ of the domain, with finitely many $C^1$ formulas on closed pieces of every compact subset. The normalized Lipschitz constant of $b$ is bounded absolutely, independently of its minimum, and a.e. $$|Da|\\leq C(a+1)(1+\\log(a+1))^2.\n \\label{lt:a-derivative}$$ If all the incident constants in a mesh neighborhood are one, $T$ is the identity on its inner simplices.*\n\n\n*Let $G$ parametrize finitely many compact $m$-traces, $m\\in\\{1,2\\}$, by finite closed-piece $C^1$ formulas, and suppose their intersections with the $Z_{\\sigma,I}$ are regular transverse hits away from higher ties and simplex boundaries. Every counted hit is in the interior of each counted regular $m$-dimensional parameter branch; parameter boundaries and lower-dimensional parameter strata avoid the counted dual strata. As the lower bounds of $a$ tend to infinity on the measured neighborhood, $$\\int J_mD(T\\circ G)\n \\longrightarrow\n \\sum_{\\text{hits on }Z_{\\sigma,I}}\n       \\mathcal H^m(\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}\\{v_i:i\\in I\\}),\n \\label{lt:squeeze-area}$$ with multiplicity. The local convergence, and in particular its upper bound with arbitrarily small error, is uniform when other floors of $a$ are made arbitrarily larger. Continuous weights may be bounded by their simplex suprema in this conclusion.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },

These formulas define a face-preserving, locally bi-Lipschitz self-homeomorphism $T$ of the domain, with finitely many $C^1$ formulas on closed pieces of every compact subset. The normalized Lipschitz constant of $b$ is bounded absolutely, independently of its minimum, and a.e. $$|Da|\\leq C(a+1)(1+\\log(a+1))^2.\n \\label{lt:a-derivative}$$ If all the incident constants in a mesh neighborhood are one, $T$ is the identity on its inner simplices.*\n\n\n*Let $G$ parametrize finitely many compact $m$-traces, $m\\in\\{1,2\\}$, by finite closed-piece $C^1$ formulas, and suppose their intersections with the $Z_{\\sigma,I}$ are regular transverse hits away from higher ties and simplex boundaries.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-budget-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Fix generic trace data as in Lemma <a href=\"#lt:trace-slicing\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lt:trace-slicing\">22</a> and a sufficiently fine generic mesh from Lemma <a href=\"#lt:target-mesh\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lt:target-mesh\">23</a>. There is an onto locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism $H_0:\\Omega\\to\\Omega'$, locally specified by finitely many $C^1$ formulas on closed pieces, whose image lengths and areas on the measured traces are at most their tie budgets plus arbitrarily small prescribed local errors. Nonnegative continuous output weights are allowed, with the simplex-supremum budgets of Definition <a href=\"#lt:tie-budget\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lt:tie-budget\">24</a>.*\n\n*The map can be arbitrarily close to $f$, with reciprocal closeness on interior compact sets, within the mesh displacement and chosen local modification errors. It can retain smaller exact affine germs previously installed at a locally finite family of crossings and finitely many star centers, and can retain strict compact forward and inverse safety conditions, including angular cone conditions, whenever they have positive margins. For this relative usage, measured data are separated from buffered open exemptions around the protected germs: a measured trace either avoids the exempt cores, or its weight vanishes there with a buffer. Every germ required to remain exact in $H_0$ is contained in one of these buffered exemptions. The affine crossing germs installed at the newly cleaned measured hits are retained only in the intermediate map $G$ for the concentration calculation; no affine-germ condition at those hits is imposed on $T\\circ G$. The mesh and all measured configurations are fixed before the squeezing floors are selected.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lt-budget-expectation",
        "exact_quote": "For an $m$-trace with finite image measure, the meshes in Lemma <a href=\"#lt:target-mesh\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lt:target-mesh\">23</a> satisfy $$\\mathbb E\\,\\mathcal B_m(\\Gamma)\n             \\leq C\\int_K J_mD(f\\circ P),\n \\label{lt:expected-budget}$$ with parameter multiplicity understood. For continuous weights it holds with the weighted trace integral and an arbitrarily small enlargement error as the local mesh sizes tend to zero.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-curve-probability",
        "exact_quote": "For any compact rectifiable measured curve $\\Gamma$ of finite length, any $\\delta>0$, and any $e>0$, sufficiently small local mesh upper bounds make $$\\mathcal B_1(\\Gamma)\\leq C\\operatorname{length}(\\Gamma)+e\n \\label{lt:curve-high-probability}$$ hold with probability at least $1-\\delta$. The constant $C$ is independent of $\\delta,e$ and the required mesh resolution; it is universal away from enlarged patches. Prescribed summable failure probabilities and local errors can be imposed simultaneously on a countable locally finite family of curves. In a deep anisotropic lattice patch there is also a grid-unit version: rescale to unit grid aspect, estimate length there, and convert the weight back using the longest physical side, with a constant independent of the aspect ratio in those units.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-sharp-grid",
        "exact_quote": "In a deep lattice patch adapted to a fixed $k$-plane $E$, use side $d$ along $E$ and side $\\eta d$ along $E^\\perp$, where $k\\in\\{1,2\\}$. For $m=k$, the translation-averaged tie-budget coefficient on a simple $k$-vector tangent to $E$ is at most $1+C\\eta$ times its Euclidean norm. For general measured $m$-vectors, tangent contributions have constants uniform in $\\eta$, and errors have a finite constant $C_\\eta$. After a fixed linear output normalization $A$ which keeps the two subspaces orthogonal, the sharp coefficient relative to the normalized tangent length or area is at most $1+C_A\\eta$. Here $C_A$ depends only on $\\|A\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}$ and $\\|A^{-1}\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}$. The tangent bounds remain uniform in $\\eta$, while the error constants may also depend on these two norms. The grid-unit bound of Lemma <a href=\"#lt:curve-probability\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lt:curve-probability\">28</a> also has no aspect-dependent factor on tangent lengths.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lt-source-mesh",
        "exact_quote": "There are locally finite source meshes of uniform shape with arbitrarily small local upper size bounds, preserving finitely many separated deep cubical lattice patches in prescribed orientations and with prescribed common fine scales. Deep cells may remain cubes; elsewhere they are tetrahedra, with matching facet diagonals at interfaces. For an integrable nonnegative function $g$ and $m\\in\\{1,2\\}$, their sampled $m$-faces satisfy the local estimate $$\\mathbb E\\sum_F \\ell_F^{3-m}\\int_F g\\,d\\mathcal H^m\n                    \\leq C\\int g,\n \\label{lt:source-fubini}$$ with comparable local enlargements and bounded overlap understood. In particular this applies to $g=|Df|^p$ and controls the tangential derivative trace integrals. Lattice shifts can also be uniform over any prescribed macro-period formed by grouping fine cubes.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-la-approximation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega,\\Omega'\\subset\\mathbb R^3$ be bounded domains, let $1\\le p\\le2$, and let $f:\\Omega\\to\\Omega'$ be a homeomorphism in $W^{1,p}(\\Omega;\\mathbb R^3)$. For every $\\varepsilon>0$ there is a locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism $H:\\Omega\\to\\Omega'$ such that $$\\label{la:goal}\n \\int_\\Omega \\bigl(|H-f|^p+|DH-Df|^p\\bigr)<\\varepsilon.$$ In particular, the target of $H$ is exactly $\\Omega'$, and $H\\in W^{1,p}(\\Omega;\\mathbb R^3)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-la-composition-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a compact Lipschitz parameter piece of dimension $d$, and let $q_j,q_0:D\\to U$ be Lipschitz maps into an open subset of a Euclidean space. Suppose that their images lie in one compact subset of $U$, that $q_j\\to q_0$ uniformly, and that $$Dq_j\\longrightarrow Dq_0\\quad\\hbox{a.e. on }D,\n \\qquad \\sup_j\\|Dq_j\\|_{L^\\infty(D)}<\\infty.$$ Let $v:U\\to\\mathbb R^a$ be Lipschitz on a neighborhood of that compact set. Suppose there are finitely many closed pieces $C_\\nu$ covering the neighborhood and $C^1$ maps $v_\\nu$, defined on neighborhoods of $C_\\nu$, such that $v=v_\\nu$ on $C_\\nu$. Then, for every finite $r\\ge1$, $$\\label{la:composition-convergence}\n D(v\\circ q_j)\\longrightarrow D(v\\circ q_0)\n       \\quad\\hbox{in }L^r(D).$$ No rank assumption is imposed on $Dq_0$. The assertion applies separately to an edge, a face, or a volume piece.",
        "source_id": "PM-368.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-la-composition-limit",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a compact Lipschitz parameter piece of dimension $d$, and let $q_j,q_0:D\\to U$ be Lipschitz maps into an open subset of a Euclidean space. Suppose there are finitely many closed pieces $C_\\nu$ covering the neighborhood and $C^1$ maps $v_\\nu$, defined on neighborhoods of $C_\\nu$, such that $v=v_\\nu$ on $C_\\nu$. Then, for every finite $r\\ge1$, $$\\label{la:composition-convergence}\n D(v\\circ q_j)\\longrightarrow D(v\\circ q_0)\n       \\quad\\hbox{in }L^r(D).$$ No rank assumption is imposed on $Dq_0$. The assertion applies separately to an edge, a face, or a volume piece.",

        "anchor": "stmt-la-radial-blocks",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many separated compact configurations satisfying <a href=\"#la:identity-jets\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"la:identity-jets\">[la:identity-jets]</a>, and fix $l$. For sufficiently small $h$ one can retain candidate blocks with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The expected $m$-volume of discarded candidate blocks tends to zero as $h\\to0$. The union of retained blocks therefore differs from $K$ by a set whose expected measure tends to zero.*\n\n2.  *Each retained $Q$ has the core $$B_Q=a+(1-8\\gamma)(Q-a).$$ The complement consists of shape-controlled frusta of scale $lh$, one over each boundary tile. After the common budget construction and the source and target changes described below, the map on $B_Q$ is $b$, except on caps of total measure at most $C\\gamma |Q|$. Its tangential derivative on the whole core and its core boundary is bounded by $C_b$.*\n\n3.  *Every radial connector in the final shell has image length at most $C_b\\gamma h$. Nonincident mesh edges avoid the radial-angular expansions. Subsequent cap contractions multiply their length budgets by at most a fixed constant. With $\\ell=lh$, the converted shell edge cost after constant-speed parametrization is $\\ell^{m-p}\\sum_e L_e^p$, where $L_e$ is the image length. Its bound is $O_b(l)\\sum_Q|Q|+o(1)$ after the source selection and individual curve-budget bounds, with arbitrary additional transfer errors.*\n\n\n4.  *If $m=3,p=2$, include the full wedge from $a$ to each boundary tile edge among the measured surfaces. There are bounded continuous weights, fixed before the target budget mesh, for which the final connector-face areas are bounded by the weighted $H_0$-areas, up to arbitrarily small errors. After multiplication by $lh$, the expectation of their total converted budget $\\mathcal B_{\\mathrm{wedge}}$ satisfies $$\\label{la:wedge-cost}\n     \\mathbb E\\mathcal B_{\\mathrm{wedge}}\n           \\le C_b\\gamma\\sum_i |K_i|+o(1).$$ Outer ordinary edges and faces have converted cost $O_b(l)\\sum_Q|Q|+o(1)$ on these configurations.*\n\n*The construction can be performed simultaneously with the other budgeted pieces used below. In a volume block the only measured interior surfaces are its wedges. In the plane-section application, any additional graph to be fixed is assumed to have positive clearance from $K$ and its image. Taking $h$ smaller then keeps all expansion and cap supports off that graph. More precisely, write $V$ for the ambient initial map after its output motions and $q_Q:Q\\to Q$ for the radial parameter change. The controlled trace is $V\\circ q_Q$. For $m=3$ these parameter changes are ambient source self-homeomorphisms; for $m=2$ they are retained solely as section parametrizations.
