<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Domain** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here a *domain* is a nonempty connected open set.
- **Sobolev homeomorphism** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For domains $\Omega,\Lambda\subset\mathbb R^3$ and $1\le p<\infty$, a Sobolev homeomorphism is a homeomorphism $f:\Omega\to\Lambda$ whose components and weak first derivatives belong to $L^p(\Omega)$.
- **Smooth diffeomorphism** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A smooth diffeomorphism has a smooth inverse as well as a smooth forward map.
- **Fixed-target approximation** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The fixed-target version of the approximation problem asks for diffeomorphisms $f_j:\Omega\to\Lambda$ such that both $f_j-f$ and $Df_j-Df$ tend to zero in $L^p(\Omega)$.
- **Locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism has finite Lipschitz constants in both directions on sufficiently small neighborhoods; no global bound is implicit.
- **Locally finite PL convention** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A PL map or triangulation is locally finite.
- **Matrix norms** ([source](#pm-h-5)): The symbol $\|A\|$ denotes the Frobenius norm of a matrix, and $\\|A\\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ its operator norm.
- **Measure notation** ([source](#pm-h-5)): The symbols $\mathcal L^m$ and $\mathcal H^m$ denote Lebesgue and Hausdorff measure.
- **Tangential differential** ([source](#pm-h-5)): The differential along an $m$-dimensional parameter space is denoted $D_m$ when it must be distinguished from the full differential.
- **Fine value tolerance** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A *fine value tolerance* on a domain $U$ is a positive continuous function on $U$.
- **Absolute constant** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Constants called *absolute* depend only on dimension and on fixed universal reference shapes.
- **Essential tangential compatibility** ([source](#stmt-lt-compatibility)): Let $D$ be a compact polygonal disk or a convex polyhedral cell and let $v:D\to\mathbb R^d$ be Lipschitz. We say that $v$ has essential tangential compatibility if the following properties hold. 1. At almost every interior point $x$, the a.e. derivative of $v$ has essential limit $Dv(x)$ as its argument tends to $x$. 2. In radial coordinates based at an interior point, at almost every boundary parameter the derivatives in boundary directions have essential limits, from the interior as well, equal to the derivatives of the boundary restriction. The exceptional sets here are null sets on the relevant parameter manifold. An ambient null-set assertion alone is insufficient for the second condition.
- **Disk area and boundary energy** ([source](#stmt-lt-disk)): Let $h:Q\to\mathbb R^d$ be a bi-Lipschitz embedding having essential tangential compatibility, and put $$A(h)=\int_Q J_2Dh,\qquad L(h)=\int_{\partial Q}\|D_t h\|^2.$$
- **Tie budget and dual strata** ([source](#stmt-lt-tie-budget)): In a tetrahedron $\sigma$ with vertices $v_0,\ldots,v_3$, write its barycentric coordinates as $\lambda_i$. For a set $I$ of $m+1$ vertices, the associated dual stratum is $$Z_{\sigma,I}= \{\lambda_i=\max_j\lambda_j\ (i\in I),\quad \lambda_j<\max_i\lambda_i\ (j\notin I)\} \cap\mathop{\mathrm{relint}}\sigma.$$ Write a source parametrization as $P:K\to\Omega$ and its measured image as $\Gamma=f\circ P$; lengths and areas of $\Gamma$ mean integrals on $K$. For such a generic measured $m$-trace, its budget $\mathcal B_m(\Gamma)$ is the sum, over its visits to these strata, of $$\mathcal H^m(\mathop{\mathrm{conv}}\{v_i:i\in I\}),$$ with visits counted with parameter multiplicity. A weighted budget uses in addition the supremum of a prescribed nonnegative continuous weight $w$ over $\sigma$. A fixed linear change in the output may also be applied when computing the flat weights.
- **Compatible simplex squeeze** ([source](#stmt-lt-squeeze)): On each tetrahedron put $$\begin{aligned} b(\lambda)&= \min_{\varnothing\ne\rho\subseteq\sigma} \left(b_\rho+ \frac{\max_{i\notin\rho}\lambda_i}{\max_j\lambda_j}\right), \label{lt:b-definition}\\ a(\lambda)&=e^{1/b(\lambda)}-e, \qquad T_i(\lambda)= \frac{\lambda_i e^{a(\lambda)\lambda_i}} {\sum_j\lambda_j e^{a(\lambda)\lambda_j}}, \label{lt:squeeze-definition} \end{aligned}$$
- **Flat kernel collapse** ([source](#pm-h-41)): Construct a Lipschitz map $$\label{la:flat-collapse} P_0(y,z)=(y,p_y(z)):Q\longrightarrow Q$$ which is the identity on $\partial Q$ and collapses $B$ onto $S$.
- **Positive kernel collapse** ([source](#pm-h-41)): For $0<\lambda\le1$, put $$\label{la:positive-collapse} P_\lambda=(1-\lambda)P_0+\lambda\operatorname{id}.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-main-exponent-ceiling | Sobolev exponent in the general homeomorphism approximation theorem | ≤ | $2$ | The general low-exponent theorem also requires the stated lower exponent bound. |
| q-target-distance-displacement | Pointwise displacement from the reference homeomorphism | < | $\frac12\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(h(x),\mathbb R^3\setminus\Omega')$ | The displacement bound must hold at every source point. |
| q-protected-radius-decrement | Decrease of an older protected radius at a subsequent insertion stage | = | $(4n_0+4)^{-1}$ | The absolute mesh color count determines the fixed decrement. |
| q-good-patch-error-budget | Sum of allocated good-patch derivative errors | < | $\varepsilon^p/8$ | Thresholds are fixed before the final fine-mesh resolution. |
| q-library-derivative-bound | Almost-everywhere gradient norm of the installed PL map | ≤ | $M_P$ | The finite local library fixes the bound before mesh refinement; no efficient dependence on the local bi-Lipschitz constant is asserted. |
| q-bad-patch-error-budget | Exceptional cube volume multiplied by the fixed gradient bound | < | $b_P$ | Local refinement is selected after the model derivative bounds. |
| q-disk-weak-energy | Dirichlet integral of the boundary-fixing reparametrized disk | ≤ | $C\bigl(A(h)+L(h)\bigr)+\varepsilon$ | The constant is absolute; parametrization distortion may depend on the individual embedding. |
| q-disk-sharp-energy | Asymptotic Dirichlet integral in the sharp square normalization | ≤ | $2+o(1)$ | The sharp conclusion concerns the prescribed square normalization and Frobenius gradient norm. |
| q-cell-collapse-energy | Interior derivative energy after boundary-compatible collapse | ≤ | $C_{m,p,c,C}\ell \int_{\partial D}\|D_t(H\circ\sigma)\|^p+\varepsilon.$ | The strict subcritical exponent condition is essential; constants depend on the listed shape and exponent data. |
| q-target-joint-density | Joint density of physical translation and normalized relative coordinates | ≤ | $Cd^{-3}$ | The bound applies to joint integration, including the stated point-mass interpretation for fixed relative coordinates. |
| q-squeeze-parameter-gradient | Almost-everywhere gradient norm of the squeezing parameter | ≤ | $C(a+1)(1+\log(a+1))^2$ | The normalized Lipschitz bound for the auxiliary minimum is absolute and independent of its positive minimum. |
| q-thin-grid-tangent-coefficient | Translation-averaged tie-budget coefficient relative to Euclidean tangent measure | ≤ | $1+C\eta$ | The measured dimension equals the tangent-plane dimension; general error components may have aspect-dependent constants. |
| q-radial-shell-thickness | Radial shell thickness parameter | = | $l/K_0$ | The tile ratio and sufficiently large absolute constant are fixed before macro refinement. |
| q-global-derivative-error | Global derivative approximation error | ≤ | $C_p\varepsilon_0+o(1)$ | Auxiliary errors are reduced in the stated order, and uncontrolled-cell errors are summable. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-local-tolerances** [claim](#stmt-pre-local-tolerances) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-local-tolerances): Infimal Lipschitz minorants and locally finite smooth partitions allocate positive local restrictions and summable errors. -> Compatible positive tolerances for subsequent constructions.
- **step-relative-pl** [claim](#stmt-sm-relative-pl) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-relative-pl): Transport the target PL structure to the source and apply relative approximation using the pulled-back metric. -> Fine PL approximation agreeing near the protected set.
- **step-ambient-extension** [claim](#stmt-sm-local-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-local-extension): Use supported embedding deformation, identify the common image, and extend the resulting ambient map by the identity. -> Small supported ambient extension with prescribed fixed sets.
- **step-proper-degree** [claim](#stmt-sm-proper-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-proper-degree): Prove the straight homotopy proper and count positive local degrees against the reference degree. -> Bijectivity onto the exact target and a smooth inverse.
- **step-pl-smoothing** [claim](#stmt-sm-pl-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-pl-smoothing): Smooth open edges with logarithmic cutoffs, convolve across faces with variable radius, and remove homogeneous vertex singularities by spherical isotopy. -> Smooth positive-Jacobian approximation onto the original target.
- **step-adapted-mesh** [claim](#stmt-sm-mesh) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-mesh): Choose maximal dyadic cubes below a slowly varying size function, triangulate contact facets, and color the bounded interaction graph. -> Locally finite finite-shape mesh with disjoint expanded color classes.
- **step-protected-insertion** [claim](#stmt-sm-insertion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-insertion): Pull a new embedding back through the current homeomorphism, glue it to identity near protected sets, and install it by ambient extension. -> New PL core retaining smaller older cores.
- **step-bilip-pl** [claim](#stmt-sm-bilip-pl) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-bilip-pl): Install quantized affine data on good patches, select finite relative libraries for the remaining patches, and refine only after their derivative bounds are fixed. -> Strong locally finite PL approximation onto the same target.
- **step-strong-smoothing** [claim](#stmt-sm-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-bilip-pl-2): Compose strong PL approximation with fine PL smoothing and apply the triangle inequality. -> Strong smooth diffeomorphic approximation with exact target.
- **step-piecewise-compatibility** [claim](#stmt-lt-closed-pieces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-closed-pieces): Use density on overlaps and continuity of finitely many extension derivatives, separately on the interior and boundary parameter manifolds. -> Essential tangential compatibility, including the allowed composition and coning operations.
- **step-disk-parametrization** [claim](#stmt-lt-disk) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-disk): Majorize the pullback metric, uniformize a marked quadrilateral, correct the boundary, and use coordinate rigidity for the sharp case. -> Boundary-fixing energy estimate and sharp derivative convergence for nearly flat disks.
- **step-cell-collapse** [claim](#stmt-lt-collapse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-collapse): Cone the boundary self-map and use a radial profile whose central energy vanishes in the strict subcritical regime. -> Same-image parametrization with exact boundary values and scaled boundary-energy control.
- **step-clean-crossing** [claim](#stmt-lt-clean-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-clean-crossing): Cut by a thin cylinder, remove null side loops, replace the remaining meridional disk, and realize the change ambiently. -> No crossing or a standard transverse crossing, retaining one in the opposite-side case.
- **step-affine-crossing** [claim](#stmt-lt-affine-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-affine-crossing): Extend a prescribed inner affine germ across planar annuli and the complementary half-balls. -> Exact affine germ with arbitrarily small paired support.
- **step-unknot-cut** [claim](#stmt-lt-unknot-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-unknot-cut): Analyze drilled exteriors by the amalgamated fundamental group, apply a disk theorem, and restore the tube to obtain a spanning disk. -> Boundary-parallel proper arc and standard ball–arc pair.
- **step-radial-product** [claim](#stmt-lt-ray-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-ray-product): Establish the punctured-sphere group isomorphism, drill strand tubes, recognize the relative exterior product by disk cuts, and restore the tubes. -> Marked product structure and radial-projection comparison homotopy.
- **step-star-identity** [claim](#stmt-lt-star) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-star): Straighten strands sector by sector, compare marked boundary maps using the angular test, and interpolate to an inner identity map. -> Identity germ preserving strict angular and safety margins.
- **step-relative-germs** [claim](#stmt-lt-relative-germs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-relative-germs): Realize supported coordinate changes, protect smaller affine polyhedra, apply relative PL approximation, and undo the coordinates. -> Locally bi-Lipschitz finite-piece regularization with retained germs and reciprocal compact control.
- **step-generic-traces** [claim](#stmt-lt-trace-slicing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-trace-slicing): Combine Sobolev slicing, sliced Lusin properties, finite source stratification, and multiplicity area and coarea formulas. -> Generic rectifiable traces and isolated regular complementary hits.
- **step-target-mesh** [claim](#stmt-lt-target-mesh) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-target-mesh): Combine graded jittered sites and shifted lattice patches, localize Delaunay cells, and use pulling triangulations with joint translation coordinates. -> Locally finite target triangulation with controlled joint sampling law.
- **step-simplex-squeeze** [claim](#stmt-lt-squeeze) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-squeeze): Verify face compatibility and invertibility through output ratios, then use scaled softmax limits and uniform tail domination at transverse ties. -> Compatible locally bi-Lipschitz squeeze whose trace measures converge to tie budgets.
- **step-budget-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lt-budget-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-budget-transfer): Clean measured hits, regularize relatively, and squeeze only outside buffered protected cores. -> Onto locally bi-Lipschitz map with weighted measurements bounded by original tie budgets.
- **step-expected-budgets** [claim](#stmt-lt-budget-expectation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-budget-expectation): Integrate translation coarea against the joint density, multiply by primal face weights, and use bounded local candidate overlap. -> Expected weighted budgets controlled by trace integrals.
- **step-high-probability-length** [claim](#stmt-lt-curve-probability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-curve-probability): Separate low-slope graph pieces from small remainders; charge monotone crossings geometrically and apply probability estimates only to the remainders. -> Individual length bounds with a uniform retained-length coefficient.
- **step-sharp-grid** [claim](#stmt-lt-sharp-grid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-sharp-grid): Compute positive projected leader-face contributions over a periodic Kuhn grid and use degree to identify the total tangent weight. -> Nearly Euclidean tangent budget coefficient and finite error coefficients.
- **step-source-sampling** [claim](#stmt-lt-source-mesh) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lt-source-mesh): Impose altitude avoidance on sequential jittered sites and integrate sampled face traces in joint translation coordinates. -> Uniform-shape source meshes and scaled trace expectation bounds.
- **step-composition-limit** [claim](#stmt-la-composition-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-la-composition-limit): Use weak-derivative locality on parameter preimages of closed pieces and dominated convergence over the finite branch list. -> Strong convergence of composed tangential derivatives without a rank restriction.
- **step-radial-blocks** [claim](#stmt-la-radial-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-la-radial-blocks): Select generic centers and safe spokes, install exact germs, expand radial cores, attenuate critical angular area, and contract vertex caps. -> Controlled cores and small converted shell and wedge costs.
- **step-compact-jets** [claim](#stmt-la-jet-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-la-jet-selection): Truncate positive singular values, refine to compact Whitney jets, extend full-rank charts, and isolate singular models with small excess neighborhoods. -> Finite separated positive-rank compact configurations carrying almost all derivative energy.
- **step-kernel-selection** [claim](#stmt-la-kernel-selection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-la-kernel-selection): Average trace errors of fixed kernel-collapse templates and estimate transferred shell costs using model preservation and small error terms. -> Successful kernel blocks with small failed volume and converted shell costs.
- **step-cell-completion** [claim](#stmt-la-cell-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-la-cell-completion): Choose common edge parametrizations, fill uncontrolled faces using the applicable disk or collapse estimate, and collapse volumes relative to the assembled boundary map. -> Same-image bi-Lipschitz cell fillings with matching shared-face data and controlled energy.
- **step-low-exponent-assembly** [claim](#stmt-la-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-la-cell-completion-2): Parametrize rank-one and rank-two sections, pass through positive kernel collapse, complete remaining cells, and select samples with summable errors in the stated parameter order. -> Onto locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism with global strong Sobolev error tending to zero.
- **step-main-completion** [claim](#stmt-main-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-la-cell-completion-3): Apply low-exponent locally bi-Lipschitz approximation followed by strong smoothing with successively smaller errors. -> Strong smooth diffeomorphic approximation onto the fixed target.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-fixed-target-strong-approximation**: The approximating diffeomorphisms have the same target as the original homeomorphism, with strong convergence of both values and forward derivatives.
- **invariant-fine-strong-smoothing**: A locally bi-Lipschitz Sobolev homeomorphism admits smooth diffeomorphic approximation with prescribed strong error and pointwise value tolerance, retaining the exact target.
- **invariant-proper-degree-target**: Positive smooth differential together with the stated target-distance tolerance makes the approximating map a diffeomorphism onto the original target.
- **invariant-subcritical-cell-gluing**: Subcritical cell reparametrization preserves the base image and prescribed boundary data while bounding interior energy by boundary energy and an arbitrarily small error.
- **invariant-protected-budget-transfer**: Trace budgets control image measurements of an onto locally bi-Lipschitz replacement, while exact protected germs require buffered exemptions from the measurements.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-boundary-regularity**: Treat fixed-target approximation as a guarantee of smooth extension to the domain boundaries or preservation of prescribed boundary values. -> The conclusion concerns smooth maps and inverses on open domains; it supplies no boundary extension theorem.
- **anti-use-inverse-sobolev-convergence**: Infer strong Sobolev convergence of inverse maps from smooth invertibility of every approximant. -> Forward derivative approximation does not supply the claimed inverse convergence.
- **anti-use-quantitative-solver**: Use the existence proof as a numerical solver with a certified runtime or an explicit uniform distortion bound. -> The local replacement library is chosen qualitatively, and its derivative bounds can be arbitrarily large.
- **anti-use-critical-face-collapse**: Apply the subcritical radial collapse estimate to critical faces. -> The radial integrability argument requires the exponent to be strictly below the parameter dimension.
- **anti-use-topology-as-energy-estimate**: Charge the derivative cost of a qualitative topological replacement directly to its small displacement. -> Fine value approximation alone does not bound the replacement differential.
- **anti-use-unbuffered-protected-germs**: Demand both unweighted budget transfer through a protected exact germ and preservation of that germ during squeezing. -> The relative transfer statement excludes protected cores from the measurements or makes their weights vanish with a buffer.
- **anti-use-positive-jacobian-alone**: Conclude global bijectivity solely from a positive smooth Jacobian. -> The fixed-target argument additionally uses fine displacement control to establish a proper homotopy and the required global degree.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is an existence and convergence theorem for arbitrary Sobolev homeomorphisms on open domains, using locally finite constructions and qualitative topological replacement choices. It does not give a finite effective procedure for selecting the required replacements and approximation scales. The source states: “The qualitative topology used to choose those models supplies no derivative estimate.” It further states: “They may be arbitrarily large; no efficient dependence on $K_P$ is asserted.” Testing finite sampled maps or evaluating the explicit simplex formulas would cover a restricted component, without deciding the global approximation, properness, or strong convergence asserted here.

It therefore supplies an existence specification rather than a complete software implementation contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Smooth invertible deformation is relevant to simulation geometry, but the result supplies neither a finite simulation update nor a boundary animation contract. Its smoothness conclusions concern the open domains. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Fine tolerances provide a mathematical interface for localized approximation, but no tool for computing admissible tolerances from finite input is specified. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper gives explicit local squeezing formulas, but the global approximation also selects representatives through qualitative existence arguments. Those local formulas alone do not constitute an algorithm for arbitrary Sobolev homeomorphisms. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Triangulations and tie budgets are geometric proof objects. The paper does not supply a finite storage representation or operations contract for the locally finite global structures. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite local model libraries establish derivative control before mesh refinement, but they do not specify accelerator kernels, arithmetic precision, or a hardware execution model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proper-degree criterion proves geometric bijectivity of a smooth map. It provides no cryptographic transformation, adversarial model, or security guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The approximation theorem does not specify a learned representation or training procedure. In particular, smooth inverses of approximants do not establish the inverse convergence that an invertible learned deformation model might require. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction controls approximation errors through trace measurements and retained jets. It does not produce an optimizer, an objective-value certificate, or a method for solving the surrounding variational problems. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Disjoint supports permit simultaneous geometric modifications, but the paper does not define a distributed protocol, communication model, or fault recovery mechanism. |
| other (Nonlinear elasticity and mathematical deformation approximation) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | For an elastic deformation already satisfying the homeomorphism and Sobolev hypotheses, the paper constructs smooth diffeomorphic approximants with strong forward Sobolev convergence and exactly the original target. The construction proceeds through locally bi-Lipschitz approximation followed by smoothing; its qualitative choices require adaptation before numerical implementation. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-global-homeomorphism-input** (hypothesis): The general approximation theorem requires a homeomorphism between nonempty bounded connected open spatial domains with the stated forward Sobolev regularity. Blocked use: ['Apply the result directly to a discontinuous, noninjective, or nonsurjective deformation.']
- **limit-main-exponent-range** (formal_scope): The general homeomorphism approximation result concerns the low and critical exponent range. The broader exponent range of the smoothing theorem requires locally bi-Lipschitz input. Blocked use: ['Extend the general approximation theorem to higher exponents solely by citing the smoothing theorem.']
- **limit-open-domain-boundary-scope** (formal_scope): Exact preservation of the target does not assert smooth extension to either boundary or preservation of prescribed boundary values. Blocked use: ['Treat the result as a boundary extension theorem.', 'Infer pointwise boundary agreement from equality of the target domains.']
- **limit-inverse-convergence** (formal_scope): Smooth invertibility of each approximant does not establish Sobolev convergence of its inverse. Blocked use: ['Transfer the forward convergence conclusion to inverse derivatives without a separate estimate.']
- **limit-local-versus-global-distortion** (formal_scope): Local bi-Lipschitz regularity does not imply uniform forward or inverse Lipschitz bounds throughout the domain. Blocked use: ['Use local regularity as a global distortion bound near the ends of the domain.']
- **limit-proper-degree-preconditions** (hypothesis): The exact-target criterion requires bounded domains, an orientation-preserving comparison homeomorphism, a positive smooth Jacobian, and the strict target-distance displacement bound everywhere. Blocked use: ['Conclude global bijectivity from a positive Jacobian alone.', 'Replace the global pointwise displacement condition by agreement at finitely many sampled points.']
- **limit-relative-pl-protected-region** (hypothesis): Relative PL approximation preserves a closed protected region only when the input is already PL on a neighborhood of that region. The boundary version additionally requires protection of the manifold boundary. Blocked use: ['Demand exact retention of arbitrary non-PL protected data through the relative PL approximation step.']
- **limit-topological-extension-not-energy** (nonconstructive): Qualitative topological replacement supplies no derivative estimate. Small displacement alone cannot pay the derivative cost of an inserted replacement. Blocked use: ['Bound replacement energy solely by the size of its displacement.', 'Choose exceptional volumes before fixing the replacement derivative bounds.']
- **limit-model-library-effectivity** (nonconstructive): Finite local model libraries give finite derivative bounds without an asserted efficient dependence on local bi-Lipschitz constants. Blocked use: ['Advertise an effective runtime, uniform distortion estimate, or computable approximation rate from the library existence argument.']
- **limit-essential-parameter-compatibility** (hypothesis): Disk parametrization and cell collapse require essential tangential compatibility on the actual parameter manifolds. An ambient null-set assertion does not provide the required boundary directional limits. Blocked use: ['Pull an ambient almost-everywhere derivative statement through a rank-deficient parametrization as a substitute for parameter-space compatibility.']
- **limit-sharp-disk-regime** (hypothesis): The sharp disk conclusion requires the specified square normalization, bounded boundary energy, nearly minimal area, and uniform convergence of boundary values. Parametrization distortion may depend on the individual embedding. Blocked use: ['Infer sharp derivative convergence from small image area alone.', 'Infer a uniform bi-Lipschitz constant for the chosen disk parametrizations.']
- **limit-collapse-strict-subcriticality** (edge_case): Radial cell collapse requires the exponent to be strictly below the parameter dimension. Critical faces use the separate disk estimate. Blocked use: ['Apply planar radial collapse at the critical exponent.', 'Discard the cell-shape and boundary-compatibility hypotheses when filling uncontrolled cells.']
- **limit-crossing-cleaning-scope** (hypothesis): Crossing cleaning assumes a locally flat surface disk, a straight interval in supplied coordinates, an isolated interior intersection, and absence of other relevant sheets nearby. Retention of a crossing follows under the opposite-side condition. Blocked use: ['Apply the cleaning assertion to accumulated intersections or intersecting sheets without establishing its local hypotheses.', 'Assume every isolated contact must survive as a transverse crossing.']
- **limit-star-angular-tests** (hypothesis): Identity-germ replacement at a star center requires the separated labelled cone systems, nested single-crossing sections, cardinality conditions, and an applicable angular test. It imposes no identity condition at other points of the original intervals. Blocked use: ['Infer identity-germ replacement from conical containment alone.', 'Require the modification to fix the remaining original ray intervals pointwise.']
- **limit-generic-trace-selection** (hypothesis): Trace rectifiability, multiplicity area formulas, and regular complementary intersections are obtained for generic translated templates. Surface traces are admitted at the critical exponent. Blocked use: ['Assume arbitrary predetermined traces satisfy the sliced Lusin and intersection conclusions.', 'Use surface-area budgets below the critical exponent without additional justification.']
- **limit-joint-sampling-law** (formal_scope): The target mesh estimate controls a joint density of physical translation and relative coordinates. Its use does not assert a bounded normalized conditional translation density after fixing the relative coordinates. Blocked use: ['Normalize a translation slice after conditioning and reuse the joint density bound unchanged.']
- **limit-squeeze-asymptotic-measurements** (approximation): Simplex squeezing converts measurements to tie budgets asymptotically for finite regular trace configurations with transverse interior hits away from higher ties and simplex boundaries. Blocked use: ['Identify a finite squeezing parameter with the limiting flat-face measurement.', 'Apply the concentration assertion to boundary hits or nontransverse intersections without regularization.']
- **limit-protected-germ-exemptions** (hypothesis): Exact germs retained by budget transfer must lie in buffered exemptions from the measurements. Newly cleaned measured crossings retain affine germs only in the intermediate map, rather than necessarily after squeezing. Blocked use: ['Demand unweighted transfer through an exempt exact-germ core.', 'Require squeezing to retain the intermediate affine germ at every measured crossing.']
- **limit-expectation-versus-sample** (approximation): Expected budget estimates do not by themselves certify every sample. Individual curve bounds require sufficiently fine meshes and permit prescribed failure probabilities. Blocked use: ['Treat an expectation bound as a deterministic bound for an arbitrary selected mesh.', 'Assume pointwise convergence of lattice intersection counts on arbitrary measured traces.']
- **limit-thin-grid-error-constants** (hypothesis): Thin-grid tangent bounds remain uniform in the aspect parameter, while error components may carry aspect-dependent constants. Output normalization must preserve the stated orthogonality. Blocked use: ['Apply the sharp tangent coefficient to arbitrary error components.', 'Shrink the aspect parameter after fixing errors without accounting for the resulting constants.']
- **limit-radial-block-choice-order** (hypothesis): Radial blocks require separated compatible compact jets. Critical wedge weights and clearance margins are fixed before budget transfer, while attenuation parameters are chosen afterward from the actual finite configuration. Blocked use: ['Choose measurement weights after selecting the budgeted map.', 'Allow additional measured interior surfaces to bypass the wedge and clearance requirements.', 'Interpret section parametrizations as additional ambient source changes.']
- **limit-compact-jets-and-kernel-tests** (hypothesis): Positive-rank compact jets carry almost all derivative energy after truncation. Kernel-block success depends on trace tests and on shrinking excess neighborhoods after the finite inflation constants are fixed. Blocked use: ['Discard positive-rank derivative energy without accounting for the tail.', 'Charge aspect-dependent or template-dependent constants to the unrestricted energy tail.', 'Assume every candidate kernel block succeeds.']
- **limit-composition-closed-piece-regularity** (hypothesis): The tangential composition limit assumes compact image containment, uniform convergence, almost-everywhere convergence and uniform bounds for parameter derivatives, and finitely many closed-piece smooth formulas for the outer map. Blocked use: ['Apply the composition conclusion to an arbitrary Lipschitz outer map without establishing the finite closed-piece hypotheses.', 'Infer strong derivative convergence from uniform convergence of parameter maps alone.']
- **limit-shared-cell-boundary-data** (hypothesis): Uncontrolled-cell completion requires compatible face-preserving boundary self-maps agreeing on shared edges. Critical prescribed edge restrictions must have essential derivative limits almost everywhere. Blocked use: ['Choose neighboring face fillings independently with incompatible shared boundary values.', 'Infer that preservation of each base image alone guarantees a continuous global assembly.']
- **limit-global-summability** (hypothesis): Local constructions yield global Sobolev approximation only after derivative costs and uncontrolled-cell errors are made summable. Value convergence also uses the fixed bounded target. Blocked use: ['Replace global Sobolev membership by local membership in the final approximation claim.', 'Transfer the value-convergence argument to an unbounded target without a separate integrable bound.']

          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lt:star-replacement\" label=\"lt:star-replacement\"></span>Use centered Euclidean coordinates in the source and target of a local homeomorphism $h$, with $h(0)=0$. Let $$D=\\{v_1,\\ldots,v_n\\}\\subset S^2,\\qquad n\\geq3,\n \\qquad R_i=\\{t v_i:t\\geq0\\},$$ be distinct labelled directions, partitioned into forward and reverse sets $F$ and $E$. The tolerance also keeps the image loops outside the cone caps.           "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal T$ be a locally finite triangulation of a three-dimensional open domain. Assign constants $0<b_\\sigma\\leq1$ to its tetrahedra, and to every nonempty face $\\rho$ assign the minimum of the constants of its incident tetrahedra. On each tetrahedron put $$\\begin{aligned}\n b(\\lambda)&=\n \\min_{\\varnothing\\ne\\rho\\subseteq\\sigma}\n \\left(b_\\rho+\n        \\frac{\\max_{i\\notin\\rho}\\lambda_i}{\\max_j\\lambda_j}\\right),\n       \\label{lt:b-definition}\\\\\n a(\\lambda)&=e^{1/b(\\lambda)}-e,\n \\qquad\n T_i(\\lambda)=\n \\frac{\\lambda_i e^{a(\\lambda)\\lambda_i}}\n             {\\sum_j\\lambda_j e^{a(\\lambda)\\lambda_j}},\n       \\label{lt:squeeze-definition}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where the maximum over an empty set in <a href=\"#lt:b-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"lt:b-definition\">[lt:b-definition]</a> is zero. These formulas define a face-preserving, locally bi-Lipschitz self-homeomorphism $T$ of the domain, with finitely many $C^1$ formulas on closed pieces of every compact subset. The normalized Lipschitz constant of $b$ is bounded absolutely, independently of its minimum, and a.e. $$|Da|\\leq C(a+1)(1+\\log(a+1))^2.\n \\label{lt:a-derivative}$$ If all the incident constants in a mesh neighborhood are one, $T$ is the identity on its inner simplices.*\n\n\n*Let $G$ parametrize finitely many compact $m$-traces, $m\\in\\{1,2\\}$, by finite closed-piece $C^1$ formulas, and suppose their intersections with the $Z_{\\sigma,I}$ are regular transverse hits away from higher ties and simplex boundaries. Every counted hit is in the interior of each counted regular $m$-dimensional parameter branch; parameter boundaries and lower-dimensional parameter strata avoid the counted dual strata. As the lower bounds of $a$ tend to infinity on the measured neighborhood, $$\\int J_mD(T\\circ G)\n \\longrightarrow\n \\sum_{\\text{hits on }Z_{\\sigma,I}}\n       \\mathcal H^m(\\mathop{\\mathrm{conv}}\\{v_i:i\\in I\\}),\n \\label{lt:squeeze-area}$$ with multiplicity. The local convergence, and in particular its upper bound with arbitrarily small error, is uniform when other floors of $a$ are made arbitrarily larger. Write $g$ for the prescribed or subsequently chosen boundary data. On every prescribed core face $F$, assume that $g|_F=v|_F\\circ s_F$, where $s_F:F\\to F$ is a bi-Lipschitz self-homeomorphism preserving each edge. These self-maps agree on shared edges. When $p=2$, their edge restrictions have derivatives with essential limits almost everywhere. Then the cell can be reparametrized bi-Lipschitzly, preserving its base image and all prescribed boundary data, with derivative cost at most a constant times $$\\label{la:cell-cost}\n \\begin{split}\n H\\sum_{F\\text{ prescribed}}\\int_F|D_tg|^p\n &+H^2\\sum_{e\\subset\\partial D}\\int_e|D_tg|^p\\\\\n &+\\mathbf1_{\\{p=2\\}}\\,\n       H\\sum_{F\\text{ uncontrolled}}\\operatorname{Area}(v|_F)\n \\end{split}$$ plus any prescribed positive error. An unprescribed edge of length comparable to $H$ may be given energy at most $C\\operatorname{length}(v(e))^pH^{1-p}$. The constants depend only on $p$ and the fixed shape bounds, and adjacent cells can be completed with identical shared-face data.",

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We resolve the three-dimensional Ball–Evans approximation problem for $`1\le p\le2`$. Every $`W^{1,p}`$ homeomorphism between arbitrary bounded domains in ℝ<sup>3</sup> is a strong $`W^{1,p}`$ limit of smooth diffeomorphisms onto the same target.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction and conventions

The Ball–Evans approximation problem concerns the compatibility of Sobolev approximation with global injectivity. A deformation in nonlinear elasticity is represented by a map between spatial domains; injectivity expresses noninterpenetration, whereas its weak first derivative records the local deformation. Smooth approximation is elementary if injectivity is discarded, but convolution need not preserve it. The question is whether the two requirements can be met simultaneously. Ball’s formulation explicitly attributes the question to Evans (Ball 2001, sec. 3, p. 8) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Ball2001](#ref-Ball2001)]; his later discussion and Hencl’s survey explain the surrounding variational questions (Ball 2010; Hencl 2026) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Ball2010](#ref-Ball2010), [Hencl2025](#ref-Hencl2025)].

Here a *domain* is a nonempty connected open set. For domains $\Omega,\Lambda\subset\mathbb R^3$ and $1\le p<\infty$, a Sobolev homeomorphism is a homeomorphism $f:\Omega\to\Lambda$ whose components and weak first derivatives belong to $L^p(\Omega)$. A smooth diffeomorphism has a smooth inverse as well as a smooth forward map. The fixed-target version of the approximation problem asks for diffeomorphisms $f_j:\Omega\to\Lambda$ such that both $f_j-f$ and $Df_j-Df$ tend to zero in $L^p(\Omega)$. In particular, every approximating map, not just the limit, must have image exactly $\Lambda$.

We resolve this problem for the low and critical exponents in dimension three.

<a id="stmt-main-theorem"></a>
<div id="main:theorem" class="theorem">

<a id="main:convergence"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *Let $\Omega,\Lambda\subset\mathbb R^3$ be nonempty bounded connected open sets, let $1\le p\le2$, and let $f:\Omega\to\Lambda$ be a homeomorphism in $W^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb R^3)$. There exist $C^\infty$ diffeomorphisms $f_j:\Omega\to\Lambda$, each belonging to $W^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb R^3)$, such that $$\label{main:convergence}
\int_\Omega\bigl( |f_j-f|^p+|Df_j-Df|^p\bigr)\,dx\longrightarrow0.$$*

</div>

Both the map and its inverse in the conclusion are smooth on their open domains. No smoothness at the boundary, boundary extension of $f$, or regularity of either boundary is assumed. Neither inverse Sobolev regularity nor a nonvanishing Jacobian is an additional hypothesis. The target $\Lambda$ is fixed throughout.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Prior work and the three-dimensional difficulty

In the plane, Iwaniec, Kovalev, and Onninen proved strong diffeomorphic approximation for $1<p<\infty$ (Iwaniec, Kovalev, and Onninen 2011, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[IwaniecKovalevOnninen2011](#ref-IwaniecKovalevOnninen2011)]. Hencl and Pratelli established the endpoint $p=1$, including approximation by locally finite piecewise affine homeomorphisms (Hencl and Pratelli 2018, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[HenclPratelli2018](#ref-HenclPratelli2018)]. These theorems preserve the original target and do not require Sobolev regularity of the inverse. They show that the global topological constraint can be compatible with strong derivative control throughout the planar exponent range. The endpoint is a distinct achievement: strict convexity of the $L^p$ norm is unavailable at $p=1$.

The planar development also distinguishes two mechanisms. The $p=2$ precursor of Iwaniec, Kovalev, and Onninen used harmonic replacements and smoothing (Iwaniec, Kovalev, and Onninen 2012) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[IwaniecKovalevOnninen2012](#ref-IwaniecKovalevOnninen2012)]; their general $p>1$ result used coordinatewise $p$-harmonic replacements. Hencl and Pratelli’s endpoint proof instead used geometric extension estimates and an adapted grid. Quantitative approximation of bi-Lipschitz planar maps by Daneri and Pratelli (Daneri and Pratelli 2014) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[DaneriPratelli2014](#ref-DaneriPratelli2014)], and approximation of planar bi-Sobolev $W^{1,1}$ maps by Pratelli (Pratelli 2017) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Pratelli2017](#ref-Pratelli2017)], additionally control inverse derivatives under their respective stronger hypotheses. Smooth invertibility of each approximant in Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a> is not a claim of Sobolev convergence of the inverses.

Higher dimensions exhibit a genuine obstruction. Hencl and Vejnar initiated the counterexamples at $p=1$ (Hencl and Vejnar 2016) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[HenclVejnar2016](#ref-HenclVejnar2016)]. Campbell, Hencl, and Tengvall corrected a gap in that construction and extended the range to $1\le p<\lfloor n/2\rfloor$ in dimensions $n\ge4$, with Jacobian determinants of both signs on sets of positive measure (Campbell, Hencl, and Tengvall 2018) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CampbellHenclTengvall2018](#ref-CampbellHenclTengvall2018)]. A sequence of diffeomorphisms cannot reproduce this sign pattern by strong convergence of its derivatives. In dimension three, Hencl and Malý’s orientation theorem instead gives $\det Df\ge0$ almost everywhere for a sense-preserving $W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}$ homeomorphism (Hencl and Malý 2010, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[HenclMaly2010](#ref-HenclMaly2010)]. This sign conclusion does not assert a positive Jacobian and does not furnish an approximation: rank-deficient derivatives still require separate constructions.

Smoothing a homeomorphism that is already piecewise affine is an important intermediate problem. Mora-Corral and Pratelli obtained fixed-image diffeomorphic smoothing in the plane, with control of the forward map and its inverse (Mora-Corral and Pratelli 2014) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[MoraCorralPratelli2014](#ref-MoraCorralPratelli2014)]. Campbell and Soudský proved $C^1$ homeomorphic approximation of piecewise affine maps in arbitrary dimension (Campbell and Soudský 2021) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[CampbellSoudsky2021](#ref-CampbellSoudsky2021)]; their conclusion does not require the inverse to be smooth. Campbell, D’Onofrio, and Vítek subsequently proved diffeomorphic smoothing of locally finite piecewise affine homeomorphisms in dimensions three and four, including forward and inverse derivative-error control (Campbell, D’Onofrio, and Vítek 2026, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[CampbellDOnofrioVitek2026](#ref-CampbellDOnofrioVitek2026)]. These results identify the importance of distinguishing a smooth injective map from a diffeomorphism. They do not construct a strong piecewise affine approximation of an arbitrary Sobolev homeomorphism.

The present proof separates that approximation problem from smoothing. It first constructs an onto locally bi-Lipschitz map with small strong Sobolev error, and then proves a smoothing theorem with arbitrarily fine pointwise control. Three-dimensional PL topology, developed by Moise and Bing and used here in Hamilton’s relative formulation, provides qualitative replacements (Moise 1952a, 1952b; Bing 1959; Hamilton 1976) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Moise1952Approximation](#ref-Moise1952Approximation), [Moise1952Triangulation](#ref-Moise1952Triangulation), [Bing1959](#ref-Bing1959), [Hamilton1976](#ref-Hamilton1976)]. It supplies no derivative estimate. The energy bounds must therefore come from the parametrizations and measurements used below, rather than from the mere existence of a topological replacement.

The critical exponent $p=2$ has additional analytic structure. Csörnyei, Hencl, and Malý proved that a three-dimensional $W^{1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}$ homeomorphism has an inverse of locally bounded variation, and that almost every parallel plane has the two-dimensional Lusin property (Csörnyei, Hencl, and Malý 2010, Theorems 1.1 and 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[CsornyeiHenclMaly2010](#ref-CsornyeiHenclMaly2010)]. We use these two statements, respectively, to select reverse rays and to measure generic image surfaces. Neither is an assumption on the given inverse. The further analytic ingredients include Whitney’s extension theorem for compatible first jets (Whitney 1934) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Whitney1934](#ref-Whitney1934)] and conformal parametrization of metric disks (L. Ahlfors and Bers 1960; McMullen 2023) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[AhlforsBers1960](#ref-AhlforsBers1960), [McMullen2023](#ref-McMullen2023)]. Their roles and the accompanying local estimates are made explicit at the point of use.

The complementary range $2<p<\infty$ is treated by a separate construction in (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[OpenAIHigh2026](#ref-OpenAIHigh2026)]. The present paper does not invoke that range theorem or any of its proof modules. The smoothing theorem proved here holds for all finite exponents; the companion also includes a complete proof in its Appendix A.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Measurements, local models, and the proof strategy

The first objective is to replace $f$ by a locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism while estimating derivatives without a quantitative topological extension theorem. We measure the images of a locally finite collection of source curves, and also of source surfaces when $p=2$. A target triangulation gives complementary-dimensional dual pieces inside its tetrahedra, defined by ties among their largest barycentric coordinates. Intersections with these pieces determine a *budget*: each curve crossing is charged the length of a corresponding target edge, and each surface crossing is charged the area of a corresponding target face, with multiplicity. Generic sampling bounds these sums by the original trace integrals. Local topological changes make the counted crossings regular; a face-preserving target reparametrization then concentrates the image length or area near them. Thus Proposition <a href="#lt:budget-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lt:budget-transfer">26</a> converts intersection counts into actual measurements for an onto locally bi-Lipschitz replacement. All protected local data are retained in buffered regions that are excluded from the measurements.

Measurements alone give an upper energy bound, not proximity to $Df$. We therefore retain finitely many disjoint compact sets on which the values and first derivatives of $f$ are controlled and the derivative has a fixed positive rank. The discarded derivative integral is small; rank-zero points carry no such energy. The construction on these compact sets depends on the rank.

1.  At full rank, compatible first jets extend to local $C^1$ diffeomorphisms. Radial source changes enlarge an exact germ, meaning agreement with such a model near the block center, into most of a small block, so that the map recovers the prescribed jet there. The surrounding thin region is filled using the trace budgets. At $p=2$, reverse-ray selection and angular attenuation control the surface cost of this radial construction (Lemma <a href="#la:radial-blocks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="la:radial-blocks">34</a>).

2.  At rank one or two, source coordinates split into the nonzero directions and the kernel of a nearby fixed matrix. Compressing the kernel reduces the controlled part of a volume block to a line or plane section. Nearly minimal curve length gives the correct derivative on a line. At rank two and $p=2$, the sharp boundary-parametrized disk estimate gives the analogous conclusion from nearly minimal area (Lemma <a href="#lt:disk" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lt:disk">14</a>). At rank two and $p<2$, a further radial construction inside the plane section provides the needed approximation.

3.  Uncontrolled cells are reparametrized relative to their already chosen boundary values. Below a cell’s dimension, radial collapse bounds its energy by the boundary energy (Lemma <a href="#lt:collapse" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lt:collapse">15</a>). This fills faces when $p<2$ and volumes throughout $1\le p\le2$; the critical faces instead use the disk estimate. The resulting maps preserve the old cell images and agree on shared faces (Lemma <a href="#la:cell-completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="la:cell-completion">37</a>).

The order of choices is part of the argument. Compact jet bounds and all finite multiplicative constants are fixed before the excess neighborhoods and trace errors are made small against them. The critical radial construction separates the regions whose area can be suppressed after replacement from those whose area must be measured in advance; Figure <a href="#la:radial-bands" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="la:radial-bands">2</a> displays these regions at the point where their construction is explained. Outside the retained compact configurations, universal mesh constants multiply only the small original energy tail. Summable local errors then yield a global $W^{1,p}$ approximation, not just a locally Sobolev map. For $p=1$, the line argument uses an elementary squared length-defect estimate followed by Hölder’s inequality, rather than strict convexity of $L^1$.

Finally, locally bi-Lipschitz maps are approximated strongly by PL maps using finite local model libraries. Their derivative bounds are fixed before reducing the exceptional mesh volume. PL smoothing then uses small edge and vertex neighborhoods with summable costs. A fine value tolerance makes the smooth map properly homotopic to the original homeomorphism inside the target; its positive local degrees and degree one force bijectivity. This proves the fixed image for each individual approximant, independently of passage to the limit.

Section <a href="#sec:smoothing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:smoothing">2</a> proves this smoothing theorem first. Section <a href="#sec:low-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:low-tools">[sec:low-tools]</a> develops the disk and collapse estimates, relative topological replacements, and sampled trace budgets. Section <a href="#sec:low-assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:low-assembly">4</a> constructs the rank-sensitive blocks and proves their global strong approximation estimate. Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">5</a> concludes the diffeomorphic approximation theorem on the original target.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Conventions

Unless stated otherwise, all domains, closures, local finiteness, and compactness are taken relative to the indicated open manifold. A compact set in an open domain has positive distance from its Euclidean complement. A PL map or triangulation is locally finite. A locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism has finite Lipschitz constants in both directions on sufficiently small neighborhoods; no global bound is implicit.

The symbol $|A|$ denotes the Frobenius norm of a matrix, and $\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ its operator norm. We use the Frobenius norm in sharp gradient estimates. Equivalent norms give the same strong convergence in Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a>. The symbols $\mathcal L^m$ and $\mathcal H^m$ denote Lebesgue and Hausdorff measure. Integrals on parameterized traces include their stated multiplicities. The differential along an $m$-dimensional parameter space is denoted $D_m$ when it must be distinguished from the full differential.

A *fine value tolerance* on a domain $U$ is a positive continuous function on $U$. To approximate finely means to meet a prescribed such tolerance pointwise. Whenever inverse control is needed on a compact localization, it is included in the prescribed tests. Locally finite constructions permit tolerances tending to zero toward the ends of the open domains; no positive global lower bound is assumed.

Constants called *absolute* depend only on dimension and on fixed universal reference shapes. A constant $C_p$ may also depend on the fixed exponent. Other dependencies are specified when the constant is introduced. A finite constant that depends on a chosen compact collection of jets or charts is fixed before an error is required to be small against it.

A homeomorphism between connected oriented domains has a constant topological orientation. If necessary, reflect the target, perform the construction for the orientation-preserving map, and undo the reflection. This convention imposes no extra hypothesis on the Sobolev differential.

<a id="stmt-pre-local-tolerances"></a>
<div id="pre:local-tolerances" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Local tolerances). *Let $U\subset\mathbb R^n$ be open and let $a:U\to(0,\infty)$ be continuous. For every $L>0$ there is a positive $L$-Lipschitz function $d$ on $U$ such that $$d(x)\le \min\{a(x),1,L\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,\mathbb R^n\setminus U)\}.$$ There are also positive smooth minorants satisfying prescribed positive continuous upper bounds on their values and first derivatives. One may additionally impose positive constant bounds on a locally finite family of compact regional tests. Finite collections of such requirements can be combined, and error budgets on a countable compact exhaustion can be made summable.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-local-tolerances"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-local-tolerances</summary>

*Proof.* Extend $e(x)=\min\{a(x),1,L\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(x,\mathbb R^n\setminus U)\}$ by zero outside $U$, and put $$d(x)=\inf_{y\in\mathbb R^n}\{e(y)+L|x-y|\}.$$ This function is $L$-Lipschitz and is bounded above by $e$. It is positive at an interior point $x$: on a small closed ball about $x$, $e$ has a positive minimum, and outside that ball the distance term has a positive lower bound.

For a smooth minorant take a smooth locally finite partition of unity $\{\chi_i\}$ with compact supports in $U$ and put $b=\sum_i c_i\chi_i$, with positive constants $c_i$. Given positive continuous bounds $v,w$, choose $$c_i\le 2^{-i-2}\min\left\{
 \inf_{\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\chi_i}v,
 \frac{\inf_{\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\chi_i}w}{1+\|D\chi_i\|_\infty}
 \right\}.$$ Then $b>0$, $b\le v$, and $|Db|\le w$. Replace a finite list of requirements by their minimum. For countably many regional tests use a locally finite compact cover with compact enlargements, refine the bounds on each intersecting support, and allocate total errors by a convergent positive series. Only finitely many regional requirements meet a given compact support. ◻

</details>

We will also use the following elementary global observation. If $H:U\to V$ is a homeomorphism and a continuous map $G:U\to V$ satisfies $$|G(x)-H(x)|<\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(H(x),\mathbb R^n\setminus V),$$ then the straight homotopy stays in $V$. When $V$ is bounded, it is a proper homotopy: the preimage of a compact target set is contained, uniformly in the homotopy parameter, in the $H$-preimage of a compact set a positive distance from $\partial V$. This observation will be used together with local injectivity and degree, not as a substitute for local injectivity.

<a id="sec:smoothing"></a>
### Qualitative topology and strong smoothing

All PL structures in this Section are the ordinary structures on open subsets of $\mathbb R^3$. Triangulations are locally finite in the open domain. In particular, neither a triangulation nor the local constants used below need have uniform behavior at the boundary.

The qualitative PL tools belong to the three-dimensional triangulation and approximation theory of Moise and Bing (Moise 1952a, 1952b; Bing 1959) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Moise1952Approximation](#ref-Moise1952Approximation), [Moise1952Triangulation](#ref-Moise1952Triangulation), [Bing1959](#ref-Bing1959)]; Hamilton’s relative formulation (Hamilton 1976) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Hamilton1976](#ref-Hamilton1976)] is the precise interface used below. Edwards–Kirby’s deformation theorem supplies the supported ambient extensions (Edwards and Kirby 1971) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[EdwardsKirby1971](#ref-EdwardsKirby1971)]. We separate these topological existence statements from the derivative estimates: finite local model libraries and subsequently chosen small exceptional sets provide the latter.

<a id="stmt-sm-smoothing"></a>
<div id="sm:smoothing" class="theorem">

<a id="sm:smoothing-conclusion"></a>
**Theorem 3** (Strong smoothing of locally bi-Lipschitz maps). *Let $\Omega,\Omega'\subset\mathbb R^3$ be bounded domains, let $1\le p<\infty$, and let $H:\Omega\to\Omega'$ be a locally bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism in $W^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb R^3)$. For every $\varepsilon>0$ and every continuous function $\xi:\Omega\to(0,\infty)$, there is a $C^\infty$ diffeomorphism $g:\Omega\to\Omega'$ such that $$\label{sm:smoothing-conclusion}
 \left\lVert g-H\right\rVert_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}<\varepsilon,
 \qquad |g(x)-H(x)|<\xi(x)\quad(x\in\Omega).$$ In particular, $g$ belongs to $W^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb R^3)$, and the approximations have exactly the target $\Omega'$.*

</div>

The proof has two parts. First we smooth a locally finite PL homeomorphism with summable local derivative errors. We then approximate $H$ by such a PL map. In the second part a finite collection of local models gives derivative bounds before the measure of the exceptional mesh cubes is made small. The qualitative topology used to choose those models supplies no derivative estimate.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Relative qualitative tools

<a id="stmt-sm-relative-pl"></a>
<div id="sm:relative-pl" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Fine relative PL approximation). *Let $M,N$ be PL three-manifolds without boundary, with $N$ metrized, and let $f:M\to N$ be a homeomorphism. Suppose that $K\subset M$ is closed and that $f$ is PL on an open neighborhood of $K$. Given a continuous function $\eta:M\to(0,\infty)$, there is a PL homeomorphism $f_1:M\to N$ which agrees with $f$ on a neighborhood of $K$ and satisfies $$d_N(f_1(x),f(x))<\eta(x)\qquad(x\in M).$$ Noncompact manifolds are allowed. For manifolds with boundary the same statement holds if $K$ contains $\partial M$ and $f$ is PL near $K$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-sm-relative-pl"><summary>Proof of stmt-sm-relative-pl</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a closed neighborhood $K^+$ of $K$ contained in the open set on which $f$ is PL. Transport the PL structure of $N$ to $M$ by $f$. The identity from the original structure to the transported structure is PL near $K^+$. Hamilton’s relative approximation Theorem (Hamilton 1976, sec. 3, Theorem 2.2, pp. 68–69) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Hamilton1976](#ref-Hamilton1976)] applies to these two structures. Use the compatible metric $d_f(x,y)=d_N(f(x),f(y))$ and the fine tolerance $\eta$. It gives a homeomorphism $a$ that is PL between the two structures, is the identity on $K^+$, and satisfies $d_f(a(x),x)<\eta(x)$. Then $f_1=f\circ a$ has all the asserted properties. The boundary condition in Hamilton’s Theorem is precisely the additional condition stated here; for an open Euclidean domain its manifold boundary is empty. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-sm-local-extension"></a>
<div id="sm:local-extension" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Small local ambient extension). *Fix a closed Euclidean cube $B\subset\mathbb R^3$, compact sets $C,D\subset B$ with $C\subset\operatorname{int}B$, and an open neighborhood $O$ of $C\cup D\cup\partial B$. For every $\lambda>0$ there is a $\delta>0$, depending only on these source sets and on $\lambda$, with the following property. If $e:O\to\mathbb R^3$ is an embedding, $$\sup_{x\in O}|e(x)-x|<\delta,
 \qquad e=\operatorname{id}\ \hbox{on }D\cup\partial B,$$ then there is a homeomorphism $A:\mathbb R^3\to\mathbb R^3$ such that <a id="sm:extension-conclusion"></a>$$\label{sm:extension-conclusion}
 A=e\ \hbox{on }C,\qquad
 A=\operatorname{id}\ \hbox{on }D\cup(\mathbb R^3\setminus\operatorname{int}B),
 \qquad \sup_{\mathbb R^3}|A-\operatorname{id}|<\lambda.$$ The number $\delta$ is uniform over a finite list of fixed normalized configurations $(B,C,D,O)$. No derivative bound on $e$ is required.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-sm-local-extension"><summary>Proof of stmt-sm-local-extension</summary>

*Proof.* Put $E=C\cup D\cup\partial B$. The deformation Theorem of Edwards–Kirby (Edwards and Kirby 1971, Theorem 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[EdwardsKirby1971](#ref-EdwardsKirby1971)], applied at the inclusion of $O$ in $\mathbb R^3$, deforms every sufficiently close embedding $e$ through embeddings $e_t$, starting at $e_0=e$, so that $e_1=\operatorname{id}$ on $E$. The deformation is unchanged outside a fixed compact neighborhood $L$ of $E$ in $O$, and fixes the inclusion. Its continuity at the inclusion permits us to require $$|e_t(x)-x|<\lambda/2\quad(x\in L,\ 0\le t\le1),
 \qquad |e(x)-x|<\lambda/2\quad(x\in L).$$ A sufficiently small uniform $\delta$ on $O$ meets the finitely many compact-open conditions that specify this neighborhood of the inclusion. In the terminology of that Theorem a proper embedding respects manifold boundaries; this condition is automatic for the ambient manifold $\mathbb R^3$.

All the open images $e_t(O)$ coincide. To see this, enclose $L$ in a finite union of relatively compact subdomains of $O$ whose boundary collars are outside the region where the deformation changes the map. The embeddings agree on that collar. Invariance of domain (Hatcher 2002, Theorem 2B.3) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Hatcher2002](#ref-Hatcher2002)] and degree relative to the common boundary show that the images of the enclosed subdomain are the same. Outside it the maps already agree. On this common open image define $$A_0=e\circ e_1^{-1},$$ and define $A_0$ to be the identity elsewhere. The map is already the identity off the compact set $e_1(L)$ in the common image, so this is an ambient homeomorphism. It agrees with $e$ on $E$ and has displacement less than $\lambda$. Since it fixes $\partial B$ pointwise, it preserves the bounded complementary component $\operatorname{int}B$. Restrict $A_0$ to $B$ and extend it by the identity outside $B$ to obtain $A$.

The construction uses only the displayed source configuration and continuity at the inclusion. For finitely many normalized configurations take the minimum of the resulting positive tolerances. ◻

</details>

<a id="sm:patched-embedding"></a>
We will use Lemma <a href="#sm:local-extension" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sm:local-extension">5</a> when the input embedding is defined by a small map $\eta$ near a new core and by the identity near older protected sets. The following observation specifies the required gluing condition. Choose open sets $\overline{O_0}\subset U_0$, where $\eta$ is defined on $U_0$, and an open set $V$ containing the protected sets, such that $\eta=\operatorname{id}$ on $U_0\cap V$. If $\eta$ is sufficiently close to the identity that $\eta(O_0)\subset U_0$, the map $$\label{sm:patched-embedding}
 e=\eta\ \hbox{on }O_0,\qquad e=\operatorname{id}\ \hbox{on }V$$ is an embedding. Indeed, a collision $\eta(x)=y$ with $x\in O_0$ and $y\in V$ has $y\in U_0\cap V$, whence $\eta(y)=y$ and injectivity of $\eta$ gives $x=y$. The open embedding property then follows from invariance of domain. All the sets in this observation can be fixed in advance when the source configurations range over a finite list.

<a id="stmt-sm-proper-degree"></a>
<div id="sm:proper-degree" class="lemma">

<a id="sm:target-distance"></a>
**Lemma 6** (Fine control, properness, and the target). *Let $h:\Omega\to\Omega'$ be an orientation-preserving homeomorphism between bounded domains, and let $g:\Omega\to\mathbb R^3$ be a smooth map with $\det Dg>0$. If $$\label{sm:target-distance}
 |g(x)-h(x)|<\frac12\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(h(x),\mathbb R^3\setminus\Omega')
 \qquad(x\in\Omega),$$ then $g$ is a diffeomorphism of $\Omega$ onto $\Omega'$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-sm-proper-degree"><summary>Proof of stmt-sm-proper-degree</summary>

*Proof.* Write $d(y)=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(y,\mathbb R^3\setminus\Omega')$ and $g_t=(1-t)h+tg$. Condition <a href="#sm:target-distance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:target-distance">[sm:target-distance]</a> puts the entire segment $g_t(x)$ in $\Omega'$. Since $d$ is 1-Lipschitz, $$d(g_t(x))\le\frac32d(h(x)).$$ If $K\Subset\Omega'$ and $m=\min_Kd>0$, the inverse image of $K$ under the homotopy lies in $$h^{-1}\bigl(\{y\in\Omega':d(y)\ge2m/3\}\bigr)\times[0,1].$$ The set in braces is compact because $\Omega'$ is bounded. Thus $g_t$ is a proper homotopy. Its endpoint $g$ has the same degree as $h$, namely one. A proper local diffeomorphism has finitely many preimages of each point, and here each preimage has positive local degree. The degree-one identity therefore says that every point of $\Omega'$ has exactly one preimage. The local smooth inverses patch to a smooth inverse on $\Omega'$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Smoothing a locally finite PL homeomorphism

<a id="stmt-sm-pl-smoothing"></a>
<div id="sm:pl-smoothing" class="proposition">

**Proposition 7** (Strong PL smoothing). *The conclusion of Theorem <a href="#sm:smoothing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sm:smoothing">3</a> holds if $H$ is replaced by a locally finite PL homeomorphism $h:\Omega\to\Omega'$ in $W^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb R^3)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-sm-pl-smoothing"><summary>Proof of stmt-sm-pl-smoothing</summary>

*Proof.* An orientation-reversing Euclidean isometry in the target reduces the proof to the orientation-preserving case. Fix a locally finite affine triangulation for $h$. We prescribe summable error budgets for its edges, vertices, and a locally finite family of compact sets covering the remainder of the domain. All modifications below can also obey an arbitrary positive continuous value tolerance.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
###### The open edges.

Around each open edge choose a tube of radius $d(t)$, where $0<t<l$ is its axial coordinate. The tubes of distinct open edges are disjoint, their radii are bounded by a small multiple of $\min(t,l-t)$, and $d$ is exactly linear near both endpoints. These choices follow from the finite geometry of each simplex star; local finiteness makes them compatible throughout $\Omega$. We may make $\left\lVert d'\right\rVert_\infty$ small by decreasing the width.

In positively oriented orthogonal frames, and after translations, the original map on this tube is <a id="sm:edge-form"></a>$$\label{sm:edge-form}
 (t,r,\theta)\longmapsto
 \bigl(at+r b(\theta),\;r s(\theta)e_{\phi(\theta)}\bigr),
 \qquad e_\alpha=(\cos\alpha,\sin\alpha),$$ where $a>0$, $s>0$, and the coefficients are smooth on the closed angular sectors. The transverse homogeneous map is injective: otherwise two equal transverse images, taken at arbitrarily small radius, would have their axial difference canceled by changing $t$, contradicting injectivity of $h$ in the edge star. Its angular map is consequently an orientation-preserving circle homeomorphism. We choose a lift $\phi$ with $\phi(\theta+2\pi)=\phi(\theta)+2\pi$. Positive determinants on the finitely many sectors give upper and positive lower bounds for $s$ and for the one-sided derivatives $\phi'$.

Choose constants $c>0$ and $c'$, and for $0\le\tau\le1$ put $$b_\tau=(1-\tau)b,\qquad
 s_\tau=(1-\tau)s+\tau c,\qquad
 \phi_\tau=(1-\tau)\phi+\tau(\theta+c').$$ For fixed $\tau$, the determinant of the corresponding map in the frame $(\partial_t,\partial_r,r^{-1}\partial_\theta)$ is <a id="sm:edge-determinant"></a>$$\label{sm:edge-determinant}
 a s_\tau^2\phi_\tau'>0.$$ On all the closed sectors and all $\tau\in[0,1]$ this has a positive minimum. Let $\chi$ be a smooth cutoff equal to 1 on $(-\infty,-1]$ and to 0 on $[-1/2,\infty)$, and substitute $$\tau(t,r)=\chi\!\left(\frac{\log(r/d(t))}{N}\right)$$ in the preceding formula. The additional derivative columns have size at most <a id="sm:edge-cutoff-error"></a>$$\label{sm:edge-cutoff-error}
 \frac{C}{N}\bigl(1+\left\lVert d'\right\rVert_\infty\bigr).$$ Indeed, $|r\partial_r\tau|\le C/N$ and $|r\partial_t\tau|\le C(r/d)|d'|/N$, while $r/d\le1$ on the support of the modification. The remaining factors come from the fixed angular data. Taking $N$ large preserves positive determinants on every sector, including one-sided limits. Near the axis the result is the nonsingular affine map $(t,r,\theta)\mapsto(at,cr e_{\theta+c'})$. Near the tube boundary it agrees with $h$.

All first derivatives are bounded by an edge-dependent constant independent of a further common decrease of $d$. The volume of the tube tends to zero under that decrease. Both its derivative error and its value error therefore have arbitrarily small $W^{1,p}$ cost. Choose these costs summably over the edges and call the result $h_1$. It is continuous and locally Lipschitz, fixes all vertices, and has the required small total error. Because the tube widths are exactly linear near endpoints, $h_1-h_1(v)$ is homogeneous of degree one on a sufficiently small ball about every vertex $v$.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
###### The faces and the punctured vertex balls.

Away from the vertices, the closed convex hull of the nearby almost-everywhere gradients of $h_1$ is contained in $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}^+(3)$ on a sufficiently small neighborhood of each point. At a smooth point this follows by continuity. At a nonsmooth point the only remaining interface is one face. If $A_-$ and $A_+$ are its two gradient traces, continuity gives agreement on the tangent plane, so $A_+-A_-$ has rank at most one with normal covector to that face. Consequently <a id="sm:face-segment"></a>$$\label{sm:face-segment}
 \det\bigl((1-t)A_-+tA_+\bigr)
 =(1-t)\det A_-+t\det A_+>0\quad(0\le t\le1).$$ The traces vary continuously on the two sides. Their nearby convex hull lies in a sufficiently small neighborhood of this compact positive-determinant segment, proving the assertion. Homogeneity makes this property uniform at relative scale on a punctured ball about a vertex: cover the unit sphere by finitely many of the corresponding neighborhoods and rescale.

<a id="sm:variable-convolution"></a>
<a id="sm:variable-convolution-derivative"></a>
Let $\rho\ge0$ be a smooth probability kernel supported in the unit ball. Away from vertices define $$\label{sm:variable-convolution}
 h_2(x)=\int_{\mathbb R^3}h_1(x+w(x)z)\rho(z)\,dz,$$ where $w$ is smooth and positive there and the integration balls lie in $\Omega$. Its derivative is $$\label{sm:variable-convolution-derivative}
 Dh_2(x)=\int Dh_1(x+w(x)z)\rho(z)\,dz
 +\int \bigl(Dh_1(x+w(x)z)z\bigr)\otimes Dw(x)\rho(z)\,dz.$$ The first integral is in the positive-determinant convex hull just described. Taking $w$ and $|Dw|$ sufficiently small locally makes the second integral a small perturbation, and hence gives $\det Dh_2>0$. Formula <a href="#sm:variable-convolution" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:variable-convolution">[sm:variable-convolution]</a> is smooth where $w>0$, as is also seen by writing its smooth variable kernel in the integration variable $y=x+w(x)z$.

On a small punctured ball at $v$ choose $w(x)=c_v|x-v|$, with $c_v>0$ sufficiently small. This is consistent with the relative-scale convex-hull condition and makes $h_2-h_1(v)$ homogeneous on a smaller ball. Set $h_2(v)=h_1(v)$ there. The required radius function exists by a locally finite partition of unity with sufficiently small positive coefficients. Near the vertices blend this function with $c_v|x-v|$ on disjoint annuli. The annular cutoff terms are bounded by a constant times $c_v$ if the competing radii are chosen at that same small scale, so both the radius and gradient restrictions survive. This is also an instance of the local minorant construction in Lemma <a href="#pre:local-tolerances" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:local-tolerances">2</a>.

Here is the global error choice. First take disjoint vertex balls so small that their volumes, multiplied by the fixed local gradient bounds to the power $p$, meet a summable error budget. On these balls the gradients in Equation <a href="#sm:variable-convolution-derivative" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sm:variable-convolution-derivative">[sm:variable-convolution-derivative]</a> are bounded independently of a further decrease of their radii. On a compact set outside the balls, the local gradients are bounded and converge at every piecewise smooth point as $w,|Dw|\to0$. Dominated convergence therefore gives an arbitrarily small derivative error on that compact set. A locally finite compact covering with summable budgets makes the total $W^{1,p}$ error as small as prescribed. The value error follows from local Lipschitz continuity and the small radius. These arguments include $p=1$.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
###### The vertices.

Center a homogeneous vertex ball at the origin and subtract $h_1(v)$. Write the resulting map as $F$. Euler’s identity is $DF(x)x=F(x)$. Since $DF(x)$ is invertible for $x\ne0$, $F(x)$ never vanishes there. We can thus write <a id="sm:vertex-form"></a>$$\label{sm:vertex-form}
 F(r\theta)=rR(\theta)\Phi(\theta),\qquad
 R>0,\quad\theta\in S^2.$$ Its positive determinant implies that $\Phi:S^2\to S^2$ is a smooth orientation-preserving local diffeomorphism. It is a covering by compactness, and it is one-sheeted because $S^2$ is simply connected.

Smale’s Theorem gives a smooth isotopy $\Phi_\tau$ from $\Phi$ to a rotation, smooth jointly in $(\tau,\theta)$ (Smale 1959, Theorem 6) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Smale1959](#ref-Smale1959)]; for the jointly smooth deformation see also (Li and Watts 2011, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[LiWatts2011](#ref-LiWatts2011)]. Interpolate $R$ through positive functions $R_\tau$ to a positive constant. For fixed $\tau$ the homogeneous map $F_\tau(r\theta)=rR_\tau(\theta)\Phi_\tau(\theta)$ has determinant $$R_\tau(\theta)^3 J_{S^2}\Phi_\tau(\theta)>0.$$ The family has bounded first derivatives and a positive determinant minimum, by compactness. On an arbitrarily small ball replace $F$ by $F_{\tau(r)}$, where $\tau$ is 0 near the outer boundary, 1 near the center, and $\sup_r|r\tau'(r)|$ is sufficiently small. Such a transition is obtained by spreading a fixed cutoff over a sufficiently long interval of $\log r$. Its extra derivative term is bounded by $C|r\tau'(r)|$, so positive determinant persists. At the center the map is a dilation followed by a rotation. It is therefore smooth and nonsingular there.

The derivative bound for this last modification depends on the fixed spherical isotopy but not on the support radius. Shrinking that radius gives arbitrarily small summable $W^{1,p}$ costs over all vertices. The resulting map $g$ is smooth and has positive determinant throughout $\Omega$.

Finally, in each of the three operations impose local value tolerances whose sum is less than $$\min\!\left\{\xi(x),\frac12
 \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(h(x),\mathbb R^3\setminus\Omega')\right\}.$$ The preceding constructions permit these fine tolerances and a total Sobolev error less than $\varepsilon$. Lemma <a href="#sm:proper-degree" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sm:proper-degree">6</a> makes $g$ a diffeomorphism onto the original target. This proves the Proposition. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-auto-remark-1"></a>
<div class="remark">

*Remark 8*. Theorem A of Campbell–D’Onofrio–Vítek (Campbell, D’Onofrio, and Vítek 2026) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[CampbellDOnofrioVitek2026](#ref-CampbellDOnofrioVitek2026)] also gives diffeomorphic approximation of locally finite piecewise affine homeomorphisms in dimension three. Proposition <a href="#sm:pl-smoothing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sm:pl-smoothing">7</a> records the specific local and fine-control construction needed here; the reduction from a general locally bi-Lipschitz map is proved next.

</div>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Fine meshes and controlled patch insertion

It remains to approximate $H$ strongly by a PL homeomorphism. Near most points, an affine first-order model will control the new derivative. On the remaining patches, qualitative topology provides replacements but gives no bound for their slopes. Choosing those replacements only after making the exceptional patches small would therefore leave the energy estimate circular. We instead select finite libraries of replacements on each coarse neighborhood, fixing their derivative bounds before the final mesh resolution. The mesh and insertion lemmas below let neighboring replacements retain exactly the same data on their overlaps.

<a id="sm:coarse-bilip"></a>
Fix a coarse, locally finite Whitney decomposition $\mathcal P$ of $\Omega$ into closed dyadic cubes with disjoint interiors. Choose relatively compact enlarged neighborhoods $U_P$ that are still locally finite. Enlarge them a fixed finite number of times when necessary. Since $H$ is locally bi-Lipschitz, there are constants $K_P\ge1$ such that $$\label{sm:coarse-bilip}
 K_P^{-1}|x-y|\le |H(x)-H(y)|\le K_P|x-y|
 \qquad(x,y\in U_P).$$ Enlarge $K_P$ also to bound $|DH|$ almost everywhere on $U_P$ in the chosen matrix norm. The constants may incorporate the data from finitely many neighboring coarse cubes. All references below to data local to $P$ allow this finite enlargement, but never an enlargement depending on the final fine-mesh resolution.

<a id="stmt-sm-mesh"></a>
<div id="sm:mesh" class="lemma">
